“Well, say that we don’t try to make any more trouble; that we cooperate in the examination and give you whatever information we can,” Costigan suggested. “Then you will probably be willing to give us a ship and let us go back to our own world?”
“You will not be allowed to cause any more trouble,” the amphibian declared, coldly. “Your cooperation will not be required. We will take from you whatever knowledge and information we wish. In all probability you will never be allowed to return to your own system, because as specimens you are too unique to lose. But enough of this idle chatter﻿—take them back to their quarters!”
Back to their three intercommunicating rooms the prisoners were led under heavy guard; and, true to his word, Nerado made certain that they had no more opportunities to escape. To Nevia the spaceship sped without incident, and in manacles the Terrestrials were taken to the College of Science, there to undergo the physical and psychical examinations which Nerado had promised them.
Nor had the Nevian scientist-captain erred in stating that their cooperation was neither needed nor desired. Furious but impotent, the human beings were studied in laboratory after laboratory by the coldly analytical, unfeeling scientists of Nevia, to whom they were nothing more or less than specimens; and in full measure they came to know what it meant to play the part of an unknown, lowly organism in a biological research. They were photographed, externally and internally.

===Chinese===

三根青铜触手穿过刘易斯顿贪婪的光束伸了过来，把它们砸得粉碎，又像砸不断的镣铐，把三个地球人的装甲身形捆了个结实。那机器——或生物——拖着这三件无助的货色穿门而出，进了主走廊，一路前行。不多时，三个地球人手无寸铁、身无盔甲、几乎衣不蔽体，站在了管制室里，再次面对平静如水、纹丝不动的内拉多。让性急的科斯蒂根意外的是，这位内维亚指挥官竟毫无怨怼之意。
“对自由的渴望，或许是一切有生之灵所共有。”他透过变换器评道。“不过，正如我先前所说，你们是要交由理学院研究的标本，无论你们做什么，都必被研究。认命吧。”
“那么，假如我们不再捣乱，配合检验，还把我们知道的都告诉你们，”科斯蒂根讨价，“那你们大概就愿意给条船，放我们回自己的世界了？”
“你们将不被允许再捣任何乱。”两栖动物冷冷宣告。“你们的配合，既不需要，也不想要。我们想从你们身上取什么知识、什么情报，自会去取。你们极可能永远不被允许返回自己的星系，因为作为标本，你们太独一无二，丢不得。闲话到此——把他们带回住处！”
俘虏们被重兵押回了那三间彼此相通的屋子；内拉多说到做到，再没给他们任何逃跑的机会。飞船一路无话，直抵内维亚，三个地球人戴着镣铐被送进理学院，去受内拉多发话许诺过的生理与心理检验。
内维亚这位科学家舰长说他们的配合既不需要也不想要，这话也没说错。三个地球人怒火中烧却无能为力，被内维亚那些冷冰冰、纯分析、无动于衷的科学家在一间间实验室里翻来覆去地研究；在他们眼里，三个地球人不多不少，就是标本。在一个生物学研究里充当一株无名而低等的生物是什么滋味，他们也十足地尝到了。他们被拍了照，从外到里。

===Original===

Every bone, muscle, organ, vessel, and nerve was studied and charted. Every reflex and reaction was noted and discussed. Meters registered every impulse and recorders filmed every thought, every idea, and every sensation. Endlessly, day after day, the nerve-wracking torture went on, until the frantic subjects could bear no more. White-faced and shaking, Clio finally screamed wildly, hysterically, as she was being strapped down upon a laboratory bench; and at the sound Costigan’s nerves, already at the breaking point, gave way in an outburst of berserk fury.
The man’s struggles and the girl’s shrieks were alike futile, but the surprised Nevians, after a consultation, decided to give the specimens a vacation. To that end they were installed, together with their Earthly belongings, in a three-roomed structure of transparent metal, floating in the large central lagoon of the city. There they were left undisturbed for a time﻿—undisturbed, that is, except by the continuous gaze of the crowd of hundreds of amphibians which constantly surrounded the floating cottage.
“First we’re bugs under a microscope,” Bradley growled, “then we’re goldfish in a bowl. I don’t know that.﻿ ﻿…”
He broke off as two of their jailers entered the room. Without a word into the transformers they seized Bradley and Clio. As those tentacular arms stretched out toward the girl, Costigan leaped. A vain attempt. In midair the paralyzing beam of the Nevians touched him and he crashed heavily to the crystal floor; and from that floor he looked on in helpless, raging fury while his sweetheart and his captain were carried out of their prison and into a waiting submarine.

===Chinese===

每一块骨头、每一根肌肉、每一个器官、每一条血管、每一束神经，都被查了个遍、绘成了图。每一个反射、每一次反应，都被记下、被讨论。仪表记下每一次冲动，记录仪把每一个念头、每一个念头里的每一丝感受都拍成胶片。这折磨神经的酷刑日复一日、无休无止，直到几个受难者发狂般再也支撑不住。克利奥脸色惨白、浑身发抖，在被绑上实验台的那一刻终于歇斯底里地失声尖叫；这一声，把科斯蒂根本已绷到断点的神经彻底压断，他狂暴地炸了。
男人的挣扎和姑娘的尖叫同样徒劳；但吃惊的内维亚人碰商一番之后，决定给这几件标本放个假。为此，他们连同地球带来的行装，被安置进一座三间的透明金属小屋，漂浮在城中央的大泻湖上。有一段日子，再没人来搅扰他们——准确地说，除了那群日夜围着这座漂浮小屋、目不转睛的几百个两栖动物。
“咱们先当显微镜下的虫子，”布拉德利咆哮，“再当鱼缸里的金鱼。我都不知道……”
两名狱卒进了屋，话被截断。他们一言不发地对着变换器，一把抓住了布拉德利和克利奥。见那触手般的胳膊朝姑娘伸去，科斯蒂根飞身扑上。徒劳。半空中，内维亚人的麻痹射线碰了他一下，他轰然砸倒在水晶地板上；就在那地板上，他怀着无助而狂燃的暴怒，眼睁睁看着心上人和船长被抬出牢笼，抬进一艘候着的潜艇。

## XVI Super-Ship in Action / 十六 超级飞船出战
===Original===

Doctor Frederick Rodebush sat at the control panel of Triplanetary’s newly reconstructed super-ship; one finger poised over a small black button. Facing the unknown though the physicist was, yet he grinned whimsically at his friend.
“Something, whatever it is, is about to occur. The *Boise* is about to take off. Ready, Cleve?”
“Shoot!” laconically. Cleveland also was constitutionally unable to voice his deeper sentiments in time of stress.
Rodebush drove his finger down, and instantly over both men there came a sensation akin to a tremendously intensified vertigo; but a vertigo as far beyond the space-sickness of weightlessness as that horrible sensation is beyond mere Earthly dizziness. The pilot reached weakly toward the board, but his leaden hands refused utterly to obey the dictates of his reeling mind. His brain was a writhing, convulsive mass of torment indescribable; expanding, exploding, swelling out with an unendurable pressure against its confining skull. Fiery spirals, laced with streaming, darting lances of black and green, flamed inside his bursting eyeballs. The Universe spun and whirled in mad gyrations about him as he reeled drunkenly to his feet, staggering and sprawling. He fell. He realized that he was falling, yet he could not fall! Thrashing wildly, grotesquely in agony, he struggled madly and blindly across the room, directly toward the thick steel wall. The tip of one hair of his unruly thatch touched the wall, and the slim length of that single hair did not even bend as its slight strength brought to an instant halt the hundred-and-eighty-odd pounds of mass﻿—mass now entirely without inertia﻿—that was his body.
But finally the sheer brain power of the man began to triumph over his physical torture. By force of will he compelled his grasping hands to seize a lifeline, almost meaningless to his dazed intelligence; and through that nightmare incarnate of hellish torture he fought his way back to the control board. Hooking one leg around a standard, he made a seemingly enormous effort and depressed a red button; then fell flat upon the floor, weakly but in a wave of relief and thankfulness, as his racked body felt again the wonted phenomena of weight and of inertia. White, trembling, frankly and openly sick, the two men stared at each other in half-amazed joy.

===Chinese===

弗雷德里克·罗德布什博士坐在三行星新近重建的超级飞船的操控台前；一根手指悬在一枚黑色小按钮上。这位物理学家虽然面对的是未知，却还是朝朋友咧嘴一笑，笑得古灵精怪。
“要出事了，不管是什么。‘博伊西’号要起飞了。准备好了吗，克利夫？”
“动手！”言简意赅。克利夫兰也是天生那种危急时刻吐不出深情厚谊的人。
罗德布什手指一按，劶那间，两个人浑身上下涌起一种眩晕，被强化了千倍万倍的眩晕；可这眩晕之超越失重的太空病，正如那可怕的太空病之超越寻常的地球头晕。
领航员软软地朝操作台伸手，可那双铅灌的手全然不肯听他天旋地转的头脑调遣。他的脑子成了一团扭动痉挛的、说不出的苦痛；膨胀、爆裂，以一股不可忍受的压力顶着禁锢它的颅骨。一圈圈火的螺旋，缠着一道道黑与绿的流矢，在他鼓胀欲裂的眼球里燃烧。宇宙绕着他疯狂地旋、狂乱地转，他醉汉似的晃晃悠悠撑起身，踉跄、扑跌。他摔倒了。他明白自己在坠落，却坠不下去！他在苦楚中狂乱而怪诞地扑腾，发疯似的、瞎摸着横过房间，直奔厚厚的钢壁。他一头乱发里的一根发梢碰上了墙，那一根头发的纤细身杆竟连弯都没弯一下，它那点微力就登时刹住了一百八十多磅的质量——此刻全然没有惯性的质量——他这具身体。
可终于，这人纯粹的脑力开始压倒肉体的酷刑。凭着意志，他强令乱抓的手攥住一根救命索——他那发昏的智力几乎不明白它有什么意义；穿过那场地狱酷刑的活噩梦，他一步步打回了操控台。一条腿勾住支柱，他作了一次看似浩大无比的努力，按下一枚红色按钮；随即瘫倒在地板上，虚弱，却在一阵解脱与感激的浪涌里——他那受尽折磨的身体，重新觉出了重量与惯性这些熟稔的现象。两人脸色惨白，浑身发抖，毫不掩饰地难受着，却半是惊异地欢喜地对望。

===Original===

“It worked,” Cleveland smiled wanly as he recovered sufficiently to speak, then leaped to his feet. “Snap it up, Fred! We must be falling fast﻿—we’ll be wrecked when we hit!”
“We’re not falling anywhere.” Rodebush, foreboding in his eyes, walked over to the main observation plate and scanned the heavens. “However, it’s not as bad as I was afraid it might be. I can still recognize a few of the constellations, even though they are all pretty badly distorted. That means that we can’t be more than a couple of light-years or so away from the Solar System. Of course, since we had so little thrust on, practically all of our energy and time was taken up in getting out of the atmosphere. Even at that, though, it’s a good thing that space isn’t a perfect vacuum, or we would have been clear out of the Universe by this time.”
“Huh? What are you talking about? Impossible! Where are we, anyway? Then we must be making mil.﻿ ﻿… Oh, I see!” Cleveland exclaimed, somewhat incoherently, as he also stared into the plate.
“Right. We aren’t traveling at all﻿—*now*.” Rodebush replied. “We are perfectly stationary relative to Tellus, since we made that hop without inertia. We must have attained one hundred percent neutralization﻿—one hundred point oh oh oh oh oh﻿—which we didn’t quite expect. Therefore we must have stopped instantaneously when our inertia was restored. Incidentally, that original, pre-inertialess velocity ‘intrinsic’ velocity, suppose we could call it?﻿—is going to introduce plenty of complications, but we don’t have to worry about them right now. Also, it isn’t *where* we are that is worrying me﻿—we can get fixes on enough recognizable stars to find that out in short order﻿—it’s *when*.”
“That’s right, too. Say we’re two light years away from home. You think maybe that we’re two years older now than we were ten minutes ago? Interesting no end﻿—and distinctly possible. Maybe even probable﻿—I wouldn’t know﻿—there’s been a lot of discussion on that theory, and as far as I know we’re the first ones who ever had a chance to prove or disprove it absolutely. Let’s snap back to Tellus and find out, right now.”
“We’ll do that, after a little more experimenting. You see, I had no intention of giving us such a long push. I was going to throw the switches in and out, but you know what happened.

===Chinese===

“成了。”克利夫兰缓过劲来能开口了，惨然一笑，随即一跃而起。“麻利点，弗雷德！咱们准在飞快下坠——一着地就摔散架了！”
“咱们哪儿也没坠。”罗德布什眼里带着不祥，踱到主观测屏前扫视天穹。“不过，没到我最怕的那个地步。有几个星座我还认得出，虽然全都歪扭得厉害。这就是说，咱们离太阳系顶多两光年上下。当然，咱们推力开得那么小，能量和时间几乎全花在冲出大气层上了。饶是如此，幸亏太空不是完美真空，不然咱们这会儿早就窜出宇宙了。”
“啊？你说什么呢？不可能！咱们到底在哪儿？那咱们这速度准是每秒几百万……哦，我明白了！”克利夫兰也盯住屏板，语无伦次地喊。
“对。咱们压根没在赶路——此刻没有。”罗德布什答道。“相对地球，咱们纹丝不动，因为那一跃是无惯性的。咱们准是达到了百分之百的中和——一〇〇点〇〇〇〇〇——这可没大想到。所以惯性一恢复，咱们必是瞬间刹停的。顺带说一句，无惯性之前原有的那速度——‘固有’速度，姑且这么叫？——会惹来数不清的麻烦，不过眼下还用不着愁。而且，让我发愁的不是咱们在哪儿——认得出的星足够定位，一忽儿就能算出——是‘什么时候’。”
“这倒也是。就说咱们离家两光年。你是说，咱们现在比十分钟前老了两年？有趣得没边儿——而且大有可能。没准还是十拿九稳——我说不好——这个理论吵了多少年了，而据我所知，头一回有机会把它彻底证实或证伪的，就是咱们。这就弹回地球去看看。”
“要回去，也先再试它一试。要知道，我本没打算推这么长一程。我是想开关一合就断的，可后来的事你也见了。”

===Original===

However, there’s one good thing about it﻿—it’s worth two years of anybody’s life to settle that relativity-time thing definitely, one way or the other.”
“I’ll say it is. But say, we’ve got a lot of power on our ultra-wave; enough to reach Tellus, I think. Let’s locate the sun and get in touch with Samms.”
“Let’s work on these controls a little first, so we’ll have something to report. Out here’s a fine place to try the ship out﻿—nothing in the way.”
“All right with me. But I *would* like to find out whether I’m two years older than I think I am, or not!”
Then for four hours they put the great super-ship through her paces, just as test-pilots check up on every detail of performance of an airplane of new and radical design. They found that the horrible vertigo could be endured, perhaps in time even conquered as space-sickness could be conquered, by a strong will in a sound body; and that their new conveyance had possibilities of which even Rodebush had never dreamed. Finally, their most pressing questions answered, they turned their most powerful ultra-beam communicator toward the yellowish star which they knew to be Old Sol.
“Samms﻿ ﻿… Samms.” Cleveland spoke slowly and distinctly. “Rodebush and Cleveland reporting from the ‘Space-Eating Wampus,’ now directly in line with Beta Ursae Minoris from the sun, distance about two point two light years. It will take six bands of tubes on your tightest beam, LSV3, to reach us. Barring a touch of an unusually severe type of space-sickness, everything worked beautifully; even better than either of us dared to believe. There’s something we want to know right away﻿—have we been gone four hours and some odd minutes, or better than two years?”
He turned to Rodebush and went on:
“Nobody knows how fast this ultra-wave travels, but if it goes as fast as we did coming out it’s no creeper. I’ll give him about thirty minutes, then shoot in another.﻿ ﻿…”
But, interrupting Cleveland’s remark, the care-ravaged face of Virgil Samms appeared sharp and clear upon the plate and his voice snapped curtly from the speaker.
“Thank God you’re alive, and twice that that the ship works!” he exclaimed. “You’ve been gone four hours, eleven minutes, and forty one seconds, but never mind about abstract theorizing. Get back here, to Pittsburgh, as fast as you can drive.

===Chinese===

“不过这也有桩好处——能把相对论时间这笔账彻底算清，无论算出哪头，搭上谁两年性命都值。”
“可不是。对了，咱们这超波功率不小；我看够得着地球。找到太阳，联络萨姆斯去。”
“先把这些控制器再摸一摸，好歹有东西可汇报。这外头正是试船的好地方——四下没遮没拦。”
“我没意见。可我倒真想知道，我是不是比自己以为的老了两岁！”
随后整整四个钟头，他们把这艘超级巨舰的浑身解数考了个遍，就像试飞员对一架设计空前的新式飞机逐项核验性能。他们发现，那可怕的眩晕，凭健全躯体里的坚强意志扛得住，假以时日兴许还能像太空病一样被彻底征服；而这件新坐骑的潜力，连罗德布什也从未梦见过。最要紧的问题都有了答案之后，他们把功率最强的超波通讯器对准了那颗他们认得是老太阳的淡黄恒星。
“萨姆斯……萨姆斯。”克利夫兰说得又慢又清。“罗德布什与克利夫兰，自‘吞空兽’号（Space-Eating Wampus）报告：现位于太阳与小熊座β星连线上，距离约二点二光年。你们用最窄的波束LSV3，六组电子管，够得着我们。除了染上一点特别凶的太空病，一切运转漂亮极了；比你我敢信的还好。有件事想立刻知道——我们是走了四个钟头零几分，还是两年多？”
他转向罗德布什，接着说：
“这超波跑多快没人知道，可它只要赶上咱们出来那速度的一半，也绝不是慢家伙。我给他三十来分钟，再发一遭……”
可话音未落，弗吉尔·萨姆斯那张忧劳刻痕的脸已清晰利落地显在屏板上，他的声音从扬声器里干脆地蹦出。
“谢天谢地你们还活着，更要谢天谢地船真管用！”他喊道。“你们走了四小时十一分四十一秒。抽象理论先放一边，全速回这儿来，回匹兹堡。

===Original===

That Nevian vessel or another one like her is mopping up the city, and has destroyed half the Fleet already!”
“We’ll be back there in nine minutes!” Rodebush snapped into the transmitter. “Two to get from here to atmosphere, four from Atmosphere down to the Hill, and three to cool off. Notify the full four-shift crew﻿—everybody we’ve picked out. Don’t need anybody else. Ship, equipment, and armament are *ready*!”
“Two minutes to atmosphere? Think you can do it?” Cleveland asked, as Rodebush flipped off the power and leaped to the control panel. “You might, though, at that.”
“We could do it in less than that if we had to. We used scarcely any power at all coming out, and I’m going to use quite a lot going back,” the physicist explained rapidly, as he set the dials which would determine their flashing course.
The master switches were thrown and the pangs of inertialessness again assailed them﻿—but weaker far this time than ever before﻿—and upon their lookout plates they beheld a spectacle never before seen by eye of man. For the ultra-beam, with its heterodyned vision, is not distorted by any velocity yet attained, as are the ether-borne rays of light. Converted into light only at the plate, it showed their progress as truly as though they had been traveling at a pace to be expressed in the ordinary terms of miles per hour. The yellow star that was the sun detached itself from the firmament and leaped toward them, swelling visibly, momently, into a blinding monster of incandescence. And toward them also flung the Earth, enlarging with such indescribable rapidity that Cleveland protested involuntarily, in spite of his knowledge of the peculiar mechanics of the vessel in which they were.
“Hold it, Fred, hold it! Way ’nuff!” he exclaimed.
“I’m using only a few thousand kilograms of thrust, and I’ll cut that as soon as we touch atmosphere, long before she can even begin to heat,” Rodebush explained. “Looks bad, but we’ll stop without a jar.”
“What would you call this kind of flight, Fritz?” Cleveland asked. “What’s the opposite of ‘inert’?”
“Damned if I know. Isn’t any, I guess. Light? No﻿ ﻿… how would ‘free’ be?”
“Not bad. ‘Free’ and ‘Inert’ maneuvering, eh? OK.”
Flying “free,” then, the super-ship came from her practically infinite velocity to an almost instantaneous halt in the outermost, most tenuous layer of the Earth’s atmosphere.

===Chinese===

“那艘内维亚船——或者另一艘一模一样的——正在屠城，舰队已经折了一半！”
“九分钟后到！”罗德布什对着发报机厉声道。“两分钟从这儿到大气层，四分钟从大气层到希尔，三分钟冷却。通知全套四班人马——咱们挑定的每一个。别人一概不用。船、装备、武备，万事俱备！”
“两分钟进大气层？你觉得成吗？”罗德布什关掉动力、跃到操控台前时，克利夫兰问。“倒也未必不成。”
“真要赶，比这还能快。出来时咱们几乎没费什么动力，回去我可用大的。”物理学家一边飞快地解释，一边校定那几个决定他们闪电航线的刻度盘。
总开关合上，无惯性的苦楚再次袭来——可比以往哪一回都轻得多——而在瞭望屏上，他们看到了一双肉眼从未见过的奇景。超波连同它的外差视觉，不像以太传的光线那样会被任何已达到的速度扭曲；只在屏板上才化为光的它，把他们的行程显示得真真切切，就仿佛他们是在以寻常“英里每小时”便可表述的速度赶路。那颗作为太阳的黄星脱出天幕，朝他们跃来，眼见着、一刻刻地胀成一团灼目的白炽巨怪。地球也朝他们掷来，胀大得快得没法说，克利夫兰尽管深知这艘船的奇特机理，还是不由自主地叫出了声。
“稳住，弗雷德，稳住！够了够了！”他喊。
“我只用了几千公斤推力，一沾大气层就掐掉，离她发热还早得很呢。”罗德布什解释。“看着吓人，可咱们停的时候连一下震动都没有。”
“这种飞法你管它叫什么，弗里茨？”克利夫兰问。“‘惯性’的反义词是什么来着？”
“鬼才知道。我看没有。‘轻’？不……‘自由’怎么样？”
“不赖。‘自由’与‘惯性’机动，嗯？成。”
就这样以“自由”态飞行，超级飞船从那近乎无穷的速度，在地球大气最外层、最稀薄的一层里，几乎是瞬时地刹停。

===Original===

Her halt was but momentary. Inertia restored, she dropped at a sharp angle downward. More than dropped; she was forced downward by one full battery of projectors; projectors driven by iron-powered generators. Soon they were over the Hill, whose violet screens went down at a word.
Flaming a dazzling white from the friction of the atmosphere through which she had torn her way, the *Boise* slowed abruptly as she neared the ground, plunging toward the surface of the small but deep artificial lake below the Hill’s steel apron. Into the cold waters the spaceship dove, and even before they could close over her, furious geysers of steam and boiling water erupted as the stubborn alloy gave up its heat to the cooling liquid. Endlessly the three necessary minutes dragged their slow way into time, but finally the water ceased boiling and Rodebush tore the ship from the lake and hurled her into the gaping doorway of her dock. The massive doors of the airlocks opened, and while the full crew of picked men hurried aboard with their personal equipment, Samms talked earnestly to the two scientists in the control room.
“… and about half the fleet is still in the air. They aren’t attacking; they are just trying to keep her from doing much more damage until you can get there. How about your takeoff? We can’t launch you again﻿—the tracks are gone﻿—but you handled her easily enough coming in?”
“That was all my fault,” Rodebush admitted. “I had no idea that the fields would extend beyond the hull. We’ll take her out on the projectors this time, though, the same as we brought her in﻿—she handles like a bicycle. The projector blast tears things up a little, but nothing serious. Have you got that Pittsburgh beam for me yet? We’re about ready to go.”
“Here it is, Doctor Rodebush,” came Norma’s voice, and upon the screen there flashed into being the view of the events transpiring above that doomed city. “The dock is empty and sealed against your blast.”
“Goodbye, and power to your tubes!” came Samms’ ringing voice.
As the words were being spoken mighty blasts of power raved from the driving projectors, and the immense mass of the super-ship shot out through the portals and upward into the stratosphere.

===Chinese===

这一停不过一瞬。惯性恢复，她以一个陡峭的角度向下坠。何止是坠；整整一排投射器把她往下压——铁驱发电机驱动的投射器。不多时他们已到希尔（the Hill）上空，一声令下，希尔的紫罗兰色护屏撤了下去。
一路撕开大气、摩擦烧得灼目雪亮的“博伊西”号临近地面时骤然减速，一头扎向希尔钢裙台下那座小而深的人工湖面。飞船潜入冷水，湖水还没来得及合拢，一股股蒸汽与沸水的狂泉已然喷起——那倔强的合金正向冷却液交出自己的热量。必不可少的三分钟慢得没完没了，好在湖水终于不再翻滚，罗德布什把船从湖里拔出，掷进船坞敞开的大门。气闸的重门大开，满员精选的乘员们带着随身装备匆匆登船；萨姆斯在管制室里同两位科学家恳切地谈着。
“……舰队大约还有一半在空中。他们没有进攻；只是想拖住她、别让她再造成更大破坏，等你们赶到。起飞没问题吧？我们没法再弹射你们——弹射轨没了——不过进来那一下，你驾驭得挺轻松？”
“那全怪我。”罗德布什认账。“我没想到场会延伸到船壳之外。不过这次我们用投射器出去，同进来时一样——她好使得跟自行车似的。投射器的轰射会掠坏点东西，不碍大事。匹兹堡那边的波束给我备好了吗？我们这就动身。”
“给您，罗德布什博士。”诺玛（Norma）的声音传来，屏板上闪现出那座劫数难逃的城市上空正在上演的一幕。“船坞已清空，并做了防轰射密封。”
“再会，愿诸管电力长虹！”萨姆斯洪亮的声音传来。
话音未落，驱进投射器已轰出强大的动力巨爆，超级飞船那庞然巨躯射出闸门，直上平流层。

===Original===

Through the tenuous atmosphere the huge globe rushed with ever-mounting speed, and while the hope of Triplanetary drove eastward Rodebush studied the ever-changing scene of battle upon his plate and issued detailed instructions to the highly trained specialists manning every offensive and defensive weapon.
But the Nevians did not wait to join battle until the newcomers arrived. Their detectors were sensitive﻿—operative over untold thousands of miles﻿—and the ultra-screen of the Hill had already been noted by the invaders as the Earth’s only possible source of trouble. Thus the departure of the *Boise* had not gone unnoticed, and the fact that not even with his most penetrant rays could he see into her interior had already given the Nevian commander some slight concern. Therefore as soon as it was determined that the great globe was being directed toward Pittsburgh the fish-shaped cruiser of the void went into action.
High in the stratosphere, speeding eastward, the immense mass of the *Boise* slowed abruptly, although no projector had slackened its effort. Cleveland, eyes upon interferometer grating and spectrophotometer charts, fingers flying over calculator keys, grinned as he turned toward Rodebush.
“Just as you thought, Skipper; an ultra-band pusher. C4V63L29. Shall I give him a little pull?”
“Not yet; let’s feel him out a little before we force a closeup. We’ve got plenty of mass. See what he does when I put full push on the projectors.”
As the full power of the Tellurian vessel was applied the Nevian was forced backward, away from the threatened city, against the full drive of her every projector. Soon, however, the advance was again checked, and both scientists read the reason upon their plates. The enemy had put down reenforcing rods of tremendous power. Three compression members spread out fanwise behind her, bracing her against a low mountainside, while one huge tractor beam was thrust directly downward, holding in an unbreakable grip a cylinder of earth extending deep down into bedrock.
“Two can play at that game!” and Rodebush drove down similar beams, and forward-reaching tractors as well. “Strap yourselves in solid, everybody!” he sounded in general warning. “Something is going to give way somewhere soon, and when it does we’ll get a jolt!”
And the promised jolt did indeed come soon.

===Chinese===

巨球在稀薄的大气里越冲越快；三行星的希望一路东驰，罗德布什研究着屏板上瞬息万变的战局，向把守每一件攻防武器的训练有素的专家们下达细则。
可内维亚人并不等新来的对手到了才接战。他们的探测器灵敏——射程万里不言——而希尔的超波护屏早被入侵者记下，认作地球唯一可能的麻烦源。于是“博伊西”号的出发没有逃过他们的眼睛，而连他穿透力最强的射线也照不进她内部这一事实，已经让内维亚指挥官生了几分挂心。因此，一旦查明那巨球正驶向匹兹堡，这艘鱼形的虚空巡洋舰便动手了。
高空平流层里，一路东驰的“博伊西”号猛然减速，可没有一台投射器松过劲。克利夫兰两眼盯住干涉光栅和分光光度图，手指在计算器键上翻飞，扭头朝罗德布什一咧嘴。
“全让你说着了，船长；一台超波段推进器。C4V63L29。要不要我给他来点拉力？”
“先不；逼近身之前先摸摸他的底。咱们质量管够。瞧瞧我给投射器上满推力他怎么办。”
地球舰全功率一压，内维亚船顶着自家全部投射器的全力，被顶得连连后退，离那座受难之城越来越远。可不多时，挺进又被扼住，两位科学家在屏板上读出了缘由。敌人打下了威力惊人的加固撑杆。三根承压杆在她身后成扇面张开，把她抵在一道低矮的山坡上；另有一道巨大的牵引光束直插地下，以砸不断的铁握攢住一根直透基岩的土柱。
“这个游戏两个人玩得！”罗德布什随之打下同样的力杆，外加前伸的牵引束。“各位把自己捆结实了！”他向全舰示警。“马上有什么东西要在什么地方断，断的时候咱们要吃一记猛的！”
而这记说好的猛震，果然很快就来了。

===Original===

Prodigiously massive and powerful as the Nevian was, the *Boise* was even more massive and more powerful; and as the already enormous energy feeding the tractors, pushers, and projectors was raised to its inconceivable maximum, the vessel of the enemy was hurled upward, backward; and that of Earth shot ahead with a bounding leap that threatened to strain even her mighty members. The Nevian anchor rods had not broken; they had simply pulled up the vast cylinders of solid rock that had formed their anchorages.
“Grab him now!” Rodebush yelled, and even while an avalanche of falling rock was burying the countryside Cleveland snapped a tractor ray upon the flying fish and pulled tentatively.
Nor did the Nevian now seem averse to coming to grips. The two warring super-dreadnoughts darted toward each other, and from the invader there flooded out the dread crimson opacity which had theretofore meant the doom of all things Solarian. Flooded out and engulfed the immense globe of humanity’s hope in its spreading cloud of redly impenetrable murk. But not for long. Triplanetary’s super-ship boasted no ordinary Terrestrial defense, but was sheathed in screen after screen of ultra-vibrations: imponderable walls, it is true, but barriers impenetrable to any unfriendly wave. To the outer screen the red veil of the Nevians clung tenaciously, licking greedily at every square inch of the shielding sphere of force, but unable to find an opening through which to feed upon the steel of the *Boise*’s armor.
“Get back﻿—’way back! Go back and help Pittsburgh!” Rodebush drove an ultra communicator beam through the murk to the instruments of the Terrestrial admiral; for the surviving warships of the fleet﻿—its most powerful units﻿—were hurling themselves forward, to plunge into that red destruction. “None of you will last a second in this red field. And watch out for a violet field pretty soon﻿—it’ll be worse than this. We can handle them alone, I think; but if we can’t, there’s nothing in the System that can help us!”
And now the hitherto passive screen of the super-ship became active. At first invisible, it began to glow in fierce violet light, and as the glow brightened to unbearable intensity the entire spherical shield began to increase in size.

===Chinese===

内维亚船纵然质量骇人、力量骇人，“博伊西”号却更重、更强；而当已经巨大的、喂给牵引器、推进器和投射器的能量再拔高到不可思议的极限时，敌舰被掀得向上、向后；地球的船则一跃而前，蹦得连她那副钢筋铁骨都险些吃不住劲。内维亚的锚杆没断；它们干脆连锚一起，把那一根根实岩巨柱从地里拔了出来。
“抓他！”罗德布什大吼；滚落的岩石正把乡野掩埋，克利夫兰却已一道牵引射线咬住那条飞鱼，试探着一拉。
此刻内维亚人也不忌惮短兵相接。两艘交战的超级无畏舰朝彼此疾射而去，入侵者身上涌出那片令太阳系万物闻风丧胆的绯红浑浊。涌出，漫开，用它那红得密不透光的浊云吞没了人类希望的那颗巨球。可没能吞多久。三行星的超级飞船夸耀的可不是寻常的地球防御，而是一层又一层超振动裹身：虽是无重之墙，却是任何不怀好意的波都钻不透的屏障。内维亚人的红纱死死扒在最外层护屏上，贪婪地舐遍力球护罩的每一寸，却寻不到一个缺口，钻不进去啃“博伊西”号装甲的钢。
“退后——远远退后！回去支援匹兹堡！”罗德布什把一道超波通讯束穿透浊雾，送到地球统帅的仪器上；因为舰队幸存的战舰——那些最强大的主力——正前赴后继，要一头扎进那片红色的毁灭。“在这红场里你们谁也撑不过一秒。还要当心一片紫色的场，很快就有——比这个更凶。他们俩我们独自应付得来，我想；可万一应付不来，这星系里也没谁能帮上咱们！”
此刻，超级飞船那面一直消极的护屏活了。起初无形，继而燃起灼灼的紫光，光亮强到不可逼视时，整面球形护盾开始胀大。

===Original===

Driven outward from the super-ship as a center, its advancing surface of seething energy consumed the crimson murk as a billow of blast-furnace heat consumes the cloud of snowflakes in the air above its cupola. Nor was the red death-mist all that was consumed. Between that ravening surface and the armor skin of the *Boise* there was nothing. No debris, no atmosphere, no vapor, no single atom of material substance﻿—the first time in Terrestrial experience that an absolute vacuum had ever been attained!
Stubbornly contesting every foot of way lost, the Nevian fog retreated before the violet sphere of nothingness. Back and back it fell, disappearing altogether from all space as the violet tide engulfed the enemy vessel; but the flying fish did not disappear. Her triple screens flashed into furiously incandescent splendor and she entered unscathed that vacuous sphere, which collapsed instantly into an enormously elongated ellipsoid, at each focus a madly warring ship of space.
Then in that tube of vacuum was waged a spectacular duel of ultra-weapons﻿—weapons impotent in air, but deadly in empty space. Beams, rays, and rods of Titanic power smote crackingly against ultra-screens equally capable. Time after time each contestant ran the gamut of the spectrum with his every available ultra-force, only to find all channels closed. For minutes the terrible struggle went on, then:
“Cooper, Adlington, Spencer, Dutton!” Rodebush called into his transmitter. “Ready? Can’t touch him on the ultra, so I’m going onto the macro-bands. Give him everything you have as soon as I collapse the violet. Go!”
At the word the violet barrier went down, and with a crash as of a disrupting Universe the atmosphere rushed into the void. And through the hurricane there shot out the deadliest material weapons of Triplanetary. Torpedoes﻿—non-ferrous, ultra-screened, beam-dirigible torpedoes charged with the most effective forms of material destruction known to man. Cooper hurled his canisters of penetrating gas, Adlington his allotropic-iron atomic bombs, Spencer his indestructible armor-piercing projectiles, and Dutton his shatterable flasks of the quintessence of corrosion﻿—a sticky, tacky liquid of such dire potency that only one rare Solarian element could contain it.

===Chinese===

以超级飞船为心向外推去，那面沸腾的能量前峰吞噬着绯红的浊雾，如高炉的热浪吞掉炉顶上空气里那片雪花。被吞的还不止那红色的死雾。在那贪婪的面与“博伊西”号装甲蒙皮之间，什么也没有。没有碎屑，没有大气，没有蒸气，没有一个物质原子——地球人的经验里，绝对真空头一遭实现了！
内维亚的雾在紫色的虚无之球面前节节败退，一寸寸失地都要争上一争。它一退再退，待紫色的潮水吞没敌舰，它从整个空间里彻底消失了；可那条飞鱼没有消失。她的三层护屏闪出狂暴的白炽华彩，她安然无恙地驶入那个真空之球；真空球当即塌成一根极长的椭球，两个焦点上，各是一艘杀红了眼的太空战舰。
于是，在那根真空之管里，一场超波武器的华彩决斗开打了——那些武器在空气里无能为力，在真空里却致命无比。泰坦之力的一束束、一道道、一根根，劈啪砸在同样了得的超波护屏上。双方一次次把手里所有的超波之力沿着频谱从头扫到尾，只扫见条条信道全关。恶斗持续了几分钟，随后：
“库珀、阿德灵顿、斯宾塞、达顿！”罗德布什朝发报机喊。“预备！超波奈何不了他，我要转宏波段了。紫屏一塌，你们有什么全给他。放！”
一声令下，紫色屏障塌落，大气裹着一声宇宙崩解般的巨响灌进真空。穿过这场飓风，三行星最致命的实体武器射了出去。鱼雷——无铁、超波护体、波束制导的鱼雷，装着人类已知最有效的实体毁灭物。库珀掷出他的穿透毒气罐，阿德灵顿掷出他的同素异形铁原子弹，斯宾塞掷出他砸不坏的穿甲弹，达顿掷出他一磕就碎、盛着腐蚀之精的瓶子——那是一种黏而发腻的液体，烈到只有一种太阳系稀有元素盛得住它。

===Original===

Ten, twenty, fifty, a hundred were thrown as fast as the automatic machinery could launch them; and the Nevians found them adversaries not to be despised. Size for size, their screens were quite as capable as those of the *Boise*. The Nevians’ destructive rays glanced harmlessly from their shields, and the Nevians’ elaborate screens, neutralized at impact by those of the torpedoes, were impotent to impede their progress. Each projectile must needs be caught and crushed individually by beams of the most prodigious power; and while one was being annihilated dozens more were rushing to the attack. Then while the twisting, dodging invader was busiest with the tiny but relentless destroyers, Rodebush launched his heaviest weapon.
The macro-beams! Prodigious streamers of bluish-green flame which tore savagely through course after course of Nevian screen! Malevolent fangs, driven with such power and velocity that they were biting into the very walls of the enemy vessel before the amphibians knew that their defensive shells of force had been punctured! And the emergency screens of the invaders were equally futile. Course after course was sent out, only to flare viciously through the spectrum and to go black.
Outfought at every turn, the now frantically dodging Nevian leaped away in headlong flight, only to be brought to a staggering, crashing halt as Cleveland nailed her with a tractor beam. But the Tellurians were to learn that the Nevians held in reserve a means of retreat. The tractor snapped﻿—sheared off squarely by a sizzling plane of force﻿—and the fish-shaped cruiser faded from Cleveland’s sight, just as the *Boise* had disappeared from the communicator plates of Radio Center, back in the Hill, when she was launched. But though the plates in the control room could not hold the Nevian, she did not vanish beyond the ken of Randolph, now Communications Officer in the super-ship. For, warned and humiliated by his losing one speeding vessel from his plates in Radio Center, he was now ready for any emergency. Therefore as the Nevian fled Randolph’s spy-ray held her, automatically behind it as there was the full output of twelve special banks of iron-driven power tubes; and thus it was that the vengeful Earthmen flashed immediately along the Nevians’ line of flight.

===Chinese===

十枚、二十枚、五十枚、一百枚，自动机械发射多快就飞出多快；内维亚人发现，这些对手不可小觑。个头对个头，它们的护屏同“博伊西”号的一样了得。内维亚人的毁灭射线从它们的盾上无害地滑开；内维亚人那考究的护屏，一触即被鱼雷的屏中和，拦不住它们的去路。每一枚弹都得用威力至巨的射线单独捉住、单独碾碎；而碾碎一枚的工夫，又有几十枚扑了上来。趁那条扭身闪躲的入侵舰被这些小而难缠的杀手缠得手忙脚乱，罗德布什放出了他最重的一件武器。
宏光束！一道道蓝绿色的火焰巨带，凶蛮地撕穿内维亚一层又一层的护屏！一根根毒牙，扎得又猛又快，两栖动物还没察觉自己的壳状力屏已被洞穿，它们已咬进了敌舰的舰壁！入侵者的应急护屏同样无用。一层又一层地放出去，只在频谱上凶狠地闪亮一阵，便黑了下去。
处处落了下风，仓皇闪躲的内维亚船亡命一跃而逃，却被克利夫兰一道牵引光束钉住，踉跄着轰然刹停。可地球人这就领教了：内维亚人还备着一手退路。牵引束啪地断了——被一道嗞嗞作响的力面齐齐剪断——那条鱼形巡洋舰从克利夫兰眼前淡去，正像当初“博伊西”号下水时，从希尔无线电中心的通讯屏上消失那样。可管制室里的屏板虽锁不住内维亚船，她却逃不出伦道夫（Randolph）的知觉——超级飞船上如今的通讯官。因为，那回在无线电中心眼睁睁丢掉一艘疾驰的船，他受了教训、丢了脸，如今对任何不测都有备而来。于是，内维亚船一路逃，伦道夫的间谍射线一路咬，身后自动顶上十二组特制铁驱功率管的全部输出；复仇的地球人就这样顺着内维亚人的航线，即刻闪追了下去。

===Original===

Inertialess now, pausing briefly from time to time to enable the crew to accustom themselves to the new sensations, Triplanetary’s super-ship pursued the invader; hurtling through the void with a velocity unthinkable.
“He was easier to take than I thought he would be,” Cleveland grunted, staring into the plate.
“I thought he had more stuff, too,” Rodebush assented, “but I guess Costigan got almost everything they had. If so, with all our own stuff and most of theirs besides, we should be able to take them. Conway’s data indicated that they have only partial neutralization of inertia﻿—if it’s one hundred percent we’ll never catch them﻿—but it isn’t﻿—there they are!”
“And this time I’m going to hold her or burn out all our generators trying,” Cleveland declared, grimly. “Are you fellows down there able to handle yourselves yet? Fine! Start throwing out your cans!”
Space-hardened veterans, all, the other Tellurian officers had fought off the horrible nausea of inertialessness, just as Rodebush and Cleveland had done. Again the ravening green macro-beams tore at the flying cruiser, again the mighty frames of the two spaceships shuddered sickeningly as Cleveland clamped on his tractor rod, again the highly dirigible torpedoes dashed out with their freights of death and destruction. And again the Nevian shear-plane of force slashed at the *Boise*’s tractor beam; but this time the mighty puller did not give way. Sparkling and spitting high-tension sparks, the plane bit deeply into the stubborn rod of energy. Brighter, thicker, and longer grew the discharges as the gnawing plane drew more and more power; but in direct ratio to that power the rod grew larger, denser, and ever harder to cut. More and more vivid became the pyrotechnic display, until suddenly the entire tractor rod disappeared. At the same instant a blast of intolerable flame erupted from the *Boise*’s flank and the whole enormous fabric of her shook and quivered under the force of a terrific detonation.
“Randolph! I don’t see them! Are they attacking or running?” Rodebush demanded. He was the first to realize what had happened.
“Running﻿—fast!”
“Just as well, perhaps, but get their line. Adlington!”
“Here!”
“Good! Was afraid you were gone﻿—that was one of your bombs, wasn’t it?”
“Yes. Well launched, just inside the screens.

===Chinese===

此刻无了惯性，只不时小停片刻让乘员们适应新的感受，三行星的超级飞船追着入侵者；以一种不可思议的速度在虚空中疾驰。
“他比我料想的好拿。”克利夫兰盯着屏板咕噜道。
“我也当他家底更厚。”罗德布什附和，“不过看来科斯蒂根把他们几乎掏空了。果真如此，咱们自己的全部家当，再添上他们的大半，拿下他们该不在话下。康韦的数据说，他们的惯性中和只是部分的——要是百分之百，咱们永远追不上——可不是——他们在这儿！”
“这一回，要么咬住她，要么把咱们的发电机全烧了拉倒。”克利夫兰狠声宣告。“下面几位，手脚都缓过来了吗？好！开始甩罐头！”
个个都是太空淬炼的老兵，其余地球军官们同罗德布什和克利夫兰一样，把无惯性那可怕的晕眩硬顶了过去。贪婪的绿色宏光束再次撕向疾驰的巡洋舰；克利夫兰的牵引杆再度咬合，两艘飞船巨大的骨架再度震得人反胃；高度制导的鱼雷再度驮着死亡与毁灭扑出。内维亚的剪切力面也再度劈向“博伊西”号的牵引束；可这一次，那根力大无穷的拉杆没让。高压火花劈啪迸溅，力面深深啃进那根倔强的能量杆。啃噬的力面功率越抽越高，放电越来越亮、越粗、越长；可与那功率成正比，能量杆也越长越粗、越密、越难剪。烟火越放越艳，直到整根牵引杆蓦地消失。同一瞬间，一股不堪忍受的烈焰从“博伊西”号侧腹喷出，她那整副庞然骨架在一声可怕的爆轰之下剧震不止。
“伦道夫！看不见他们了！他们在攻还是在跑？”罗德布什喝问。他是头一个醒悟出了什么事的人。
“跑——飞快！”
“也好，兴许，不过咬住他们的航线。阿德灵顿！”
“在！”
“好！还怕你没了——那是你的一枚炸弹吧？”
“是。放得正好，正落在护屏内侧。

===Original===

Don’t see how it could have detonated unless something hot and hard struck it in the tube; it would need about that much time to explode. Good thing it didn’t go off any sooner, or none of us would have been here. As it is, Area Six is pretty well done in, but the bulkheads held the damage to Six. What happened?”
“We don’t know, exactly. Both generators on the tractor beam went out. At first, I thought that was all, but my neutralizers are dead and I don’t know what else. When the G-4’s went out the fusion must have shorted the neutralizers. They would make a mess; it must have burned a hole down into number six tube. Cleveland and I will come down, and we’ll all look around.”
Donning spacesuits, the scientists let themselves into the damaged compartment through the emergency airlocks, and what a sight they saw! Both outer and inner walls of alloy armor had been blown away by the awful force of the explosion. Jagged plates hung awry; bent, twisted and broken. The great torpedo tube, with all its intricate automatic machinery, had been driven violently backward and lay piled in hideous confusion against the backing bulkheads. Practically nothing remained whole in the entire compartment.
“Nothing much we can do here,” Rodebush said finally, through his transmitter. “Let’s go see what number four generator looks like.”
That room, although not affected by the explosion from without, had been quite as effectively wrecked from within. It was still stiflingly hot; its air was still reeking with the stench of burning lubricant, insulation, and metal; its floor was half covered by a semi-molten mass of what had once been vital machinery. For with the burning out of the generator bars the energy of the disintegrating allotropic iron had had no outlet, and had built up until it had broken through its insulation and in an irresistible flood of power had torn through all obstacles in its path to neutralization.
“Hm﻿ ﻿… m﻿ ﻿… m. Should have had an automatic shut-off﻿—one detail we overlooked,” Rodebush mused. “The electricians can rebuild this stuff here, though﻿—that hole in the hull is something else again.”
“I’ll say it’s something else,” the grizzled Chief Engineer agreed. “She’s lost all her spherical strength﻿—anchoring a tractor with this ship now would turn her inside out. Back to the nearest Triplanetary shop for us, I would say.”
“Come again, Chief!”

===Chinese===

“想不通它怎么会爆，除非管子里有什么又热又硬的东西撞了它；它炸起来正需要那么长时间。幸好没早炸，不然咱们谁也剩不下。如今六区是报废得差不多了，好在舱壁把灾情锁在了六区。出了什么事？”
“确切情况还不清楚。牵引束的两台发电机都停了。起初我以为就这些，可我的中和器也死了，别的还不知道。G-4一停，那场聚爆准是把中和器短路了。那会弄得一团糟；多半在六号发射管里烧穿了一个洞。我和克利夫兰下去，大家一起看看。”
科学家们穿上太空服，从应急气闸钻进受损的舱室——好一副景象！合金装甲的外墙内墙都被爆炸的可怕威力掠飞。参差的板片歪歪斜斜地挂着；弯的、扭的、碎的。巨大的鱼雷发射管连同全套精密的自动机械，被猛地砸得向后坐去，丑陋地堆挤在承压舱壁跟前。整间舱室里，几乎没有什么还囫囵着。
“这儿咱们干不了什么。”末了，罗德布什透过送话器说。“去看看四号发电机成了什么样。”
那一间虽然没受外面的爆炸波及，却从里面被毁得同样彻底。屋里依旧热得憋闷；空气里依旧弥漫着烧焦的润滑油、绝缘物和金属的恶臭；地板被半熔的一大摊东西盖去一半——那原是要命的机械。因为发电机的导条一烧断，正在裂变的同素异形铁的能量便没了出路，节节蓄积，直到击穿绝缘，化作一股无可抗拒的洪流，把寻路中和之途上的所有障碍撕了个穿。
“嗯……嗯……嗯。本该装个自动断流——漏掉的一个细节。”罗德布什沉吟。“这里的家伙什，电工们还造得回来——船壳上那个窟窿，可就是另一回事了。”
“可不是另一回事。”头发斑白的主任轮机长附和。“她那身球形强度全没了——如今拿这船下牵引锚，非把她自己翻个里朝外不可。依我说，回最近的三行星船厂吧。”
“您再说一遍，长！”

===Original===

Cleveland advised the engineer. “None of us would live long enough to get there. We can’t travel inertialess until the repairs are made, so if they can’t be made without very much traveling, it’s just too bad.”
“I don’t see how we could support our jacks﻿ ﻿…” the engineer paused, then went on: “If you can’t give me Mars or Tellus, how about some other planet? I don’t care about atmosphere, or about anything but mass. I can stiffen her up in three or four days if I can sit down on something heavy enough to hold our jacks and presses; but if we have to rig up space-cradles around the ship herself it’ll take a long time﻿—months, probably. Haven’t got a spare planet on hand, have you?”
“We might have, at that,” Rodebush made surprising answer. “A couple of seconds before we engaged we were heading toward a sun with at least two planets. I was just getting ready to dodge them when we cut the neutralizers, so they should be fairly close somewhere﻿—yes, there’s the sun, right over there. Rather pale and small; but it’s close, comparatively speaking. We’ll go back up into the control room and find out about the planets.”
The strange sun was found to have three large and easily located children, and observation showed that the crippled spaceship could reach the nearest of these in about five days. Power was therefore fed to the driving projectors, and each scientist, electrician, and mechanic bent to the task of repairing the ruined generators; rebuilding them to handle any load which the converters could possibly put upon them. For two days the *Boise* drove on, then her acceleration was reversed, and finally a landing was effected upon the forbidding, rocky soil of the strange world.
It was larger than the Earth, and of a somewhat stronger gravitation. Although its climate was bitterly cold, even in its short daytime, it supported a luxuriant but outlandish vegetation. Its atmosphere, while rich enough in oxygen and not really poisonous, was so rank with indescribably fetid vapors as to be scarcely breatheable. But these things bothered the engineers not at all. Paying no attention to temperature or to scenery and without waiting for chemical analysis of the air, the space-suited mechanics leaped to their tasks; and in only a little more time than had been mentioned by the chief engineer the hull and giant frame of the super-ship were as staunch as of yore.

===Chinese===

克利夫兰劝这位轮机长。“真回那儿，咱们谁也活不到地头。修好之前咱们没法无惯性赶路，所以要是不用跑多远就能修，那才叫糟透了。”
“我想不出咱们的千斤顶往哪儿搭……”轮机长顿了顿，又接下去：“你们要是给不了火星或地球，别的行星怎么样？大气我不挑，除了质量什么都行。只要能坐落到一块压得住咱们千斤顶和压机的重物上，三四天我就能把她重新紧固起来；可要是在船身四周现搭太空吊架，那可就旷日持久了——没准好几个月。手边没有富余的行星吧？”
“没准还真有。”罗德布什的回答出人意料。“接战前那么两三秒，咱们正朝一颗至少带两颗行星的太阳飞。刚要躲它们，咱们就切了中和器，所以它们该就在左近什么地方——喏，那不是那颗太阳，就在那边。是有点苍白、有点小；可比较着说，算近的。咱们回管制室去，查查那几颗行星。”
那颗陌生的太阳查明有三颗又大又好找的行星，观测表明，这艘伤残的飞船大约五天可达最近的一颗。于是动力灌进驱进投射器，每一位科学家、电工、机械师都埋头抢修那几台报废的发电机；把它们重建得换能器无论压上什么负载都扛得住。“博伊西”号轰了两天，随后加速度倒转，终于降落在那陌生世界冷峭多石的土壤上。
它比地球大，引力也略强。气候严寒，连短短的白昼也不例外，却养育着繁茂而古怪的植被。它的大气氧气虽足、也并非真有毒，却饱含一股臭得说不出的浊气，几乎没法下咽。可这些全难不倒工程人员们。温度也好景致也罢一概不顾，连大气化验也不等，穿太空服的机械师们扑上了各自的活计；只比主任轮机长所说的多花了没多少工夫，超级飞船的船壳和巨型骨架便又像往日一样坚固。

===Original===

“All right, Skipper!” came finally the welcome word. “You might try her out with a fast hop around this world before you shove off in earnest.”
Under the fierce blast of her projectors the vessel leaped ahead, and time after time, as Rodebush hurled her mass upon tractor beam or pressor, the engineers sought in vain for any sign of weakness. The strange planet half girdled and the severest tests passed flawlessly, Rodebush reached for his neutralizer switches. Reached and paused, dumbfounded, for a brilliant purple light had sprung into being upon his panel and a bell rang out insistently.
“What the hell!” Rodebush shot out an exploring beam along the detector line and gasped. He stared, mouth open, then yelled:
“*Roger* is here, rebuilding his planetoid! **Stations all**!”

===Chinese===

“妥了，船长！”终于盼来了这句话。“正式开拔前，不妨带她绕这世界快跳一圈试试。”
投射器猛烈的轰射下，飞船一跃而出；罗德布什一次次把她的质量压上牵引光束或推进光束，工程人员们左寻右觅，找不出半丝疲软。绕了半个陌生行星，最严苛的测试全数无瑕通过，罗德布什伸手去够中和器开关。手到了，却僵住，惊得目瞪口呆——操控台上骤然亮起一团灼灼的紫光，一口铃铛催命般响个不停。
“搞什么鬼！”罗德布什顺着探测线放出探察束，倒抽一口气。他张着嘴瞪了半天，随即大吼：
“罗杰在这儿，正在重建他的小行星！全员就位！”

## II The Fall of Atlantis / II 亚特兰蒂斯的沦亡
===Original===



===Chinese===

II 亚特兰蒂斯的沦亡

## Eddore / 埃多尔
===Original===

“Members of the Innermost Circle, wherever you are and whatever you may be doing, tune in!” the All-Highest broadcast. “Analysis of the data furnished by the survey just completed shows that in general the Great Plan is progressing satisfactorily. There seem to be only four planets which our delegates have not been or may not be able to control properly: Sol III, Rigel IV, Velantia III, and Palain VII. All four, you will observe, are in the other galaxy. No trouble whatever has developed in our own.
“Of these four, the first requires drastic and immediate personal attention. Its people, in the brief interval since our previous general survey, have developed nuclear energy and have fallen into a cultural pattern which does not conform in any respect to the basic principles laid down by us long since. Our deputies there, thinking erroneously that they could handle matters without reporting fully to or calling for help upon the next higher operating echelon, must be disciplined sharply. Failure, from whatever cause, can not be tolerated.
“Gharlane, as Master Number Two, you will assume control of Sol III immediately. This Circle now authorizes and instructs you to take whatever steps may prove necessary to restore order upon that planet. Examine carefully this data concerning the other three worlds which may very shortly become troublesome. Is it your thought that one or more others of this Circle should be assigned to work with you, to be sure that these untoward developments are suppressed?”
“It is not, Your Supremacy,” that worthy decided, after a time of study. “Since the peoples in question are as yet of low intelligence; since one form of flesh at a time is all that will have to be energized; and since the techniques will be essentially similar; I can handle all four more efficiently alone than with the help or cooperation of others. If I read this data correctly, there will be need of only the most elementary precaution in the employment of mental force, since of the four races, only the Velantians have even a rudimentary knowledge of its uses. Right?”
“We so read the data.” Surprisingly enough, the Innermost Circle agreed unanimously.
“Go, then. When finished, report in full.”
“I go, All-Highest. I shall render a complete and conclusive report.”

===Chinese===

“最内圈（the Innermost Circle）诸位成员，无论你们身在何处、手头在做什么，都请接入！”至高者（the All-Highest）广播道。“对刚刚完成的勘察所提供数据的分析表明，总体而言，大计划（the Great Plan）进展顺利。看来唯有四颗行星，我们的代表至今未能、或将无法妥善控制：太阳三号星（Sol III）、参宿七四号星（Rigel IV）、维兰提亚三号星（Velantia III）和帕兰七号星（Palain VII）。诸位可以看到，这四颗星全在另一个星系。我们自己的星系则没有出半点乱子。
“四星之中，第一颗需要立即施以严厉的亲自过问。自上一次全面勘察以来的短短间隙里，那颗行星上的居民已经发展出核能，并陷入了一种与我们久已奠定的基本原则毫不相合的文化形态。我们在那里的代理人误以为自己无须向上一级运作阶层充分汇报或求援便能料理事务，必须严加惩处。失败，无论出于何种原因，都不能容忍。
“加兰（Gharlane），你以二号主宰（Master Number Two）之身，即刻接管太阳三号星。本圈现在授权并指示你，采取一切必要手段恢复该行星的秩序。有关另外三个世界的资料请仔细研读——它们很可能不久也会生事。依你之见，是否应由本圈再指派一人或数人与你协同，以确保这些不利的苗头被压制下去？”
“不需要，至尊。”那位要员研究片刻后裁定。“鉴于相关各族智力尚低；鉴于每次只须激励一具血肉之躯；鉴于手段本质雷同；这四颗星我独自处理，比有旁人帮忙或合作更有效率。倘若这份资料我读得不错，动用心智力量时只需最起码的防范，因为四个种族里，只有维兰提亚人对它的用法有那么一点初步认识。对不对？”
“我们也如此解读数据。”最内圈竟一致同意。
“那就去吧。完成后作全面汇报。”
“我去了，至高者。我将提交一份完整而定论式的报告。”

## Arisia / 阿里西亚
===Original===

“We, the Elder Thinkers in fusion, are spreading in public view, for study and full discussion, a visualization of the relationships existing and to exist between Civilization and its irreconcilable and implacable foe. Several of our younger members, particularly Eukonidor, who has just attained Watchmanship, have requested instruction in this matter. Being as yet immature, their visualizations do not show clearly why Nedanillor, Kriedigan, Drounli, and Brolenteen, either singly or in fusion, have in the past performed certain acts and have not performed certain others; or that the future actions of those Moulders of Civilization will be similarly constrained.
“This visualization, while more complex, more complete, and more detailed than the one set up by our forefathers at the time of the Coalescence, agrees with it in every essential. The five basics remain unchanged. First: the Eddorians can be overcome only by mental force. Second: the magnitude of the required force is such that its only possible generator is such an organization as the Galactic Patrol toward which we have been and are working. Third: since no Arisian or any fusion of Arisians will ever be able to spearhead that force, it was and is necessary to develop a race of mentality sufficient to perform that task. Fourth: this new race, having been instrumental in removing the menace of Eddore, will as a matter of course displace the Arisians as Guardians of Civilization. Fifth: the Eddorians must not become informed of us until such a time as it will be physically, mathematically impossible for them to construct any effective counter-devices.”
“A cheerless outlook, truly,” came a somber thought.
“Not so, daughter. A little reflection will show you that your present thinking is loose and turbid. When that time comes, every Arisian will be ready for the change. We know the way. We do not know to what that way leads; but the Arisian purpose in this phase of existence﻿—this space-time continuum﻿—will have been fulfilled and we will go eagerly and joyfully on to the next. Are there any more questions?”
There were none.
“Study this material, then, each of you, with exceeding care.

===Chinese===

“我们，融合为一体的长老智者（Elder Thinkers），现将一段观想图景公之于众，供研习与充分讨论——它呈现的是文明与其势不两立、绝无和解之望的仇敌之间现存的以及日后将有的关系。我们若干较年轻的成员，尤其是刚晋升守望者之位（Watchmanship）的尤科尼多（Eukonidor），请求就此受教。他们尚未成熟，观想不出为何内达尼洛（Nedanillor）、克里迪根（Kriedigan）、德伦利（Drounli）与布罗伦廷（Brolenteen）——无论单独还是融合——过去做了某些事、又不做另一些事；也观想不出这些文明的塑造者（Moulders of Civilization）未来的行动将受同样的约束。
“这段观想图景，虽比我们先辈在并合（Coalescence）之际所立的更复杂、更完整、更细致，在一切要点上却与之一致。五条基本准则依旧不变。第一：埃多尔人（Eddorians）只能以心智之力征服。第二：所需之力如此巨大，唯一可能的产生者，正是我们一直为之努力、如今仍在努力构建的那种组织——银河巡逻队（the Galactic Patrol）。第三：既然任何阿里西亚人（Arisian）、或阿里西亚人的任何融合体，都永远无法充当那股力量的先锋，培育一个心智足以担当此任的种族，便既是往昔也是眼下的必需。第四：这个新种族既在驱除埃多尔（Eddore）之患中建功，便将顺理成章地取代阿里西亚人，成为文明的守护者。第五：在埃多尔人造出任何有效反制装置已属物理上、数学上绝无可能之前，绝不可让他们知晓我们的存在。”
“这前景实在黯淡。”一个阴郁的念头传来。
“并非如此，女儿。稍加思量你便会发现，你此刻的思维松散而浑浊。那一刻到来时，每一个阿里西亚人都将为这场转变准备就绪。我们认得那条路。我们不知道那条路通向何处；但阿里西亚人在存在的这一阶段——这个时空连续体——里的使命届时已然完成，我们将热切而欢欣地走向下一阶段。还有问题吗？”
没有了。
“那么，你们每一位都请以极度的审慎研习这份材料。

===Original===

It may be that some one of you, even a child, will perceive some facet of the truth which we have missed or have not examined fully; some fact or implication which may be made to operate to shorten the time of conflict or to lessen the number of budding Civilizations whose destruction seems to us at present to be sheerly unavoidable.”
Hours passed. Days. No criticisms or suggestions were offered.
“We take it, then, that this visualization is the fullest and most accurate one possible for the massed intellect of Arisia to construct from the information available at the moment. The Moulders therefore, after describing briefly what they have already done, will inform us as to what they deem it necessary to do in the near future.”
“We have observed, and at times have guided, the evolution of intelligent life upon many planets,” the fusion began. “We have, to the best of our ability, directed the energies of these entities into the channels of Civilization; we have adhered consistently to the policy of steering as many different races as possible toward the intellectual level necessary for the effective use of the Lens, without which the proposed Galactic Patrol cannot come into being.
“For many cycles of time we have been working as individuals with the four strongest races, from one of which will be developed the people who will one day replace us as Guardians of Civilization. Blood lines have been established. We have encouraged matings which concentrate traits of strength and dissipate those of weakness. While no very great departure from the norm, either physically or mentally, will take place until after the penultimates have been allowed to meet and to mate, a definite general improvement of each race has been unavoidable.
“Thus the Eddorians have already interested themselves in our budding Civilization upon the planet Tellus, and it is inevitable that they will very shortly interfere with our work upon the other three. These four young Civilizations must be allowed to fall. It is to warn every Arisian against well-meant but inconsidered action that this conference was called. We ourselves will operate through forms of flesh of no higher intelligence than, and indistinguishable from, the natives of the planets affected. No traceable connection will exist between those forms and us.

===Chinese===

也许你们中的某一位，哪怕是个孩子，会看出我们所遗漏或未曾细究的某个真理侧面；某项事实或隐义，或可用来缩短冲突的时间，或可减少那些依我们目前看来注定无可挽救的初生文明的数量。”
数小时过去。数日过去。无人提出批评或建议。
“那么我们认定，这段观想图景便是阿里西亚的群体心智据现有信息所能构建的最完整、最精确的版本。诸位塑造者于是将在简述既成之事后，告知我们他们认为近期还须做些什么。”
“我们观察过许多行星上智慧生命的演化，有时也施以引导。”融合体开始说。“我们已竭尽所能，把这些生灵的精力导入文明的河道；我们一贯坚持一项方针：引导尽可能多的不同种族，达到足以有效运用透镜（the Lens）的智力水平——没有透镜，设想中的银河巡逻队便无从诞生。
“许多个时间周期以来，我们一直以个人身份与四个最强的种族共事，将来取代我们担任文明守护者的那个民族，便会自其中之一孕育而出。血脉已经确立。我们鼓励那些强化力量特质、淘汰软弱特质的婚配。虽然在倒数第二代人获准相遇并结合之前，肉体或心智上都不会出现对常轨的重大偏离，但每个种族整体上的确定改进已在所难免。
“于是，埃多尔人已对我们在特卢斯星（Tellus）上初生的文明发生了兴趣，而他们很快就会染指我们在另外三颗行星上的工作，这势所必然。这四个初生文明必须任其沦陷。召开此次会议，正是为了警告每一位阿里西亚人：切勿出于善意而欠虑妄动。我们自己将借血肉之躯行事，其智力不高于、其形貌不可辨于受影响行星上的土著。那些躯壳与我们之间，将不存在任何可资追索的关联。”

===Original===

No other Arisians will operate within extreme range of any one of those four planets; they will from now on be given the same status as has been so long accorded Eddore itself. The Eddorians must not learn of us until after it is too late for them to act effectively upon that knowledge. Any chance bit of information obtained by any Eddorian must be obliterated at once. It is to guard against and to negate such accidental disclosures that our Watchmen have been trained.”
“But if all of our Civilizations go down.﻿ ﻿…” Eukonidor began to protest.
“Study will show you, youth, that the general level of mind, and hence of strength, is rising,” the fused Elders interrupted. “The trend is ever upward; each peak and valley being higher than its predecessor. When the indicated level has been reached﻿—the level at which the efficient use of the Lens will become possible﻿—we will not only allow ourselves to become known to them; we will engage them at every point.”
“One factor remains obscure.” A Thinker broke the ensuing silence. “In this visualization I do not perceive anything to preclude the possibility that the Eddorians may at any time visualize us. Granted that the Elders of long ago did not merely visualize the Eddorians, but perceived them in time-space surveys; that they and subsequent Elders were able to maintain the status quo; and that the Eddorian way of thought is essentially mechanistic, rather than philosophic, in nature. There is still a possibility that the enemy may be able to deduce us by processes of logic alone. This thought is particularly disturbing to me at the present time because a rigid statistical analysis of the occurrences upon those four planets shows that they cannot possibly have been due to chance. With such an analysis as a starting point, a mind of even moderate ability could visualize us practically *in toto*. I assume, however, that this possibility has been taken into consideration, and suggest that the membership be informed.”
“The point is well taken. The possibility exists. While the probability is very great that such an analysis will not be made until after we have declared ourselves, it is not a certainty. Immediately upon deducing our existence, however, the Eddorians would begin to build against us, upon the four planets and elsewhere.

===Chinese===

其余任何阿里西亚人，一律不得进入那四颗行星中任何一颗的极远作用范围之内；从今往后，它们将与埃多尔本身一样，被置于长期同等的地位。在埃多尔人来不及据此采取有效行动之前，他们绝不能知晓我们。任何埃多尔人以任何方式偶然取得的任何一点信息，都必须立即抹除。为防范并抵消这类意外的泄露，我们的守望者才受过训练。”
“可要是我们所有的文明全都沦陷了……”尤科尼多出言抗议。
“研习之后你会明白，年轻人，心智的总体水平、因而也是力量的总体水平，正在步步上升。”融合为一的长老们打断他。“趋势永远向上；每一个高峰与低谷都高于前一个。待到所示的水平达成——也就是透镜得以有效运用的水平——我们不仅会让他们知晓我们；我们还将与他们全面交锋。”
“仍有一个因素晦暗不明。”一位智者打破随之而来的沉默。“在这段观想图景里，我看不出有什么能排除这样的可能：埃多尔人随时观想出我们。诚然，远古的长老们并非只是观想到埃多尔人，而是在时空勘察中感知到了他们；诚然，他们与其后的长老们得以维持均势至今；诚然，埃多尔人的思维方式本质上是机械论的，而非哲学的。但敌方仍有可能仅凭逻辑推演便推导出我们。这个念头此刻尤其令我不安，因为对那四颗行星上种种事件所作的一项严格统计分析表明，它们绝无可能出于偶然。以这样一项分析为起点，一个哪怕中等能力的心智，也几乎能原原本本地观想出我们。不过，我假定这一可能性已计算在内，并建议告知全体成员。”
“此点提得好。这种可能性存在。这种分析极大概率不会在我们自我宣告之前做出，但这并非定数。然而，一旦推导出我们的存在，埃多尔人便会在那四颗行星上以及其他各处，立即着手构筑对付我们的阵仗。”

===Original===

Since there is only one effective counter-structure possible, and since we Elders have long been alert to detect the first indications of that particular activity, we know that the situation remains unchanged. If it changes, we will call at once another full meeting of minds. Are there any other matters of moment﻿ ﻿… ? If not, this conference will dissolve.”

===Chinese===

既然可能奏效的反制架构只有一种，而长老们久已警觉，随时探测此项活动的最初迹象，我们知道局势依旧未变。一旦有变，我们将立即再召集一次全体心智会议。还有别的要紧事吗……？若没有，本次会议即行解散。”

## Atlantis / 亚特兰蒂斯
===Original===

Ariponides, recently elected Faros of Atlantis for his third five-year term, stood at a window of his office atop the towering Farostery. His hands were clasped loosely behind his back. He did not really see the tremendous expanse of quiet ocean, nor the bustling harbor, nor the metropolis spread out so magnificently and so busily beneath him. He stood there, motionless, until a subtle vibration warned him that visitors were approaching his door.
“Come in, gentlemen.﻿ ﻿… Please be seated.” He sat down at one end of a table molded of transparent plastic. “Psychologist Talmonides, Statesman Cleto, Minister Philamon, Minister Marxes and Officer Artomenes, I have asked you to come here personally because I have every reason to believe that the shielding of this room is proof against eavesdroppers; a thing which can no longer be said of our supposedly private television channels. We must discuss, and if possible come to some decision concerning, the state in which our nation now finds itself.
“Each of us knows within himself exactly what he is. Of our own powers, we cannot surely know each others’ inward selves. The tools and techniques of psychology, however, are potent and exact; and Talmonides, after exhaustive and rigorous examination of each one of us, has certified that no taint of disloyalty exists among us.”
“Which certification is not worth a damn,” the burly Officer declared. “What assurance do we have that Talmonides himself is not one of the ringleaders? Mind you, I have no reason to believe that he is not completely loyal. In fact, since he has been one of my best friends for over twenty years, I believe implicitly that he is. Nevertheless the plain fact is, Ariponides, that all the precautions you have taken, and any you can take, are and will be useless insofar as definite knowledge is concerned. The real truth is and will remain unknown.”
“You are right,” the Psychologist conceded. “And, such being the case, perhaps I should withdraw from the meeting.”
“That wouldn’t help, either.” Artomenes shook his head. “Any competent plotter would be prepared for this, as for any other contingency. One of us others would be the real operator.”
“And the fact that our Officer is the one who is splitting hairs so finely could be taken to indicate which one of us the real operator could be,” Marxes pointed out, cuttingly.
“Gentlemen! Gentlemen!”

===Chinese===

阿里波尼德斯（Ariponides）新近第三次当选亚特兰蒂斯（Atlantis）法罗斯（Faros），任期五年。此刻他站在高耸入云的法罗斯塔顶层、自己办公室的窗前，双手随意背在身后。那片浩瀚宁静的大洋，那繁忙的港湾，那在他脚下壮丽铺展、喧腾不息的大都会，他其实一概没有看进眼里。他一动不动地立着，直到一阵轻微的震颤提醒他，有客正朝门口走来。
“请进，诸位。……请坐。”他在一张透明塑料铸就的长桌一端落座。“心理学家塔尔莫尼德斯（Talmonides）、政治家克莱托（Cleto）、菲拉蒙部长（Philamon）、马克塞斯部长（Marxes）、统帅阿尔托梅内斯（Artomenes）：我请诸位亲自前来，是因为我有充分理由相信，这间屋子的屏蔽足以防住窃听；而我们那些名义上私密的电视频道，已经不能这么说了。我们必须讨论我们国家当前所处的境地，并尽可能做出某种决定。
“我们每个人心里都确切知道自己是什么。可单凭我们自身的能力，谁也无法确知他人的内心。然而心理学的工具与技术既有力又精确；塔尔莫尼德斯在对我们每个人做过详尽而严格的查验之后，已经证明我们之中不存在丝毫不忠的污点。”
“这证明一文不值。”魁梧的统帅断然道。“我们凭什么担保塔尔莫尼德斯本人就不是主谋之一？注意，我并没有理由认为他不完全忠诚。事实上，二十多年来他一直是我最好的朋友之一，我对此深信不疑。然而明摆着的事实是，阿里波尼德斯：你已采取的一切防范措施、以及你所能采取的任何措施，就确切的知识而言，都是、也将是无用的。真正的真相将永远无人知晓。”
“你说得对。”心理学家承认。“既然如此，也许我该退出会议。”
“那也无济于事。”阿尔托梅内斯摇头。“任何够格的阴谋家都会防到这一手，就像防其他一切变数一样。我们其余人里总有一个才是真正的主使。”
“而偏偏是我们的统帅把头发丝掰得这么细，这倒恰恰能说明，真正的主使能是谁。”马克塞斯尖刻地点破。
“诸位！诸位！”

===Original===

Ariponides protested. “While absolute certainty is of course impossible to any finite mind, you all know how Talmonides was tested; you know that in his case there is no reasonable doubt. Such chance as exists, however, must be taken, for if we do not trust each other fully in this undertaking, failure is inevitable. With this word of warning I will get on with my report.
“This worldwide frenzy of unrest followed closely upon the controlled liberation of atomic energy and may be﻿—probably is﻿—traceable to it. It is in no part due to imperialistic aims or acts on the part of Atlantis. This fact cannot be stressed too strongly. We never have been and are not now interested in Empire. It is true that the other nations began as Atlantean colonies, but no attempt was ever made to hold any one of them in colonial status against the wish of its electorate. All nations were and are sister states. We gain or lose together. Atlantis, the parent, was and is a clearing-house, a coordinator of effort, but has never claimed or sought authority to rule; all decisions being based upon free debate and free and secret ballot.
“But now! Parties and factions everywhere, even in old Atlantis. Every nation is torn by internal dissensions and strife. Nor is this all. Uighar as a nation is insensately jealous of the Islands of the South, who in turn are jealous of Maya. Maya of Bantu, Bantu of Ekopt, Ekopt of Norheim, and Norheim of Uighar. A vicious circle, worsened by other jealousies and hatreds intercrossing everywhere. Each fears that some other is about to try to seize control of the entire world; and there seems to be spreading rapidly the utterly baseless belief that Atlantis itself is about to reduce all other nations of Earth to vassalage.
“This is a bald statement of the present condition of the world as I see it. Since I can see no other course possible within the constituted framework of our democratic government, I recommend that we continue our present activities, such as the international treaties and agreements upon which we are now at work, intensifying our effort wherever possible. We will now hear from Statesman Cleto.”
“You have outlined the situation clearly enough, Faros.

===Chinese===

阿里波尼德斯摆手制止。“绝对的确定性，任何有限心智当然都不可能企及；但你们都清楚塔尔莫尼德斯当年是如何受检的；你们知道，就他而言不存在合理的怀疑。然而，哪怕仅存的这点风险也必须承担，因为在这项事业中我们若不彼此完全信任，失败便在所难免。说完这句警语，我接着往下讲。
“这场席卷全球的狂躁不安，紧跟着受控原子能的释放而来，也许——多半——正可归因于它。它与亚特兰蒂斯方面的任何帝国主义图谋或行径绝无半点干系。这一点再怎么强调都不为过。我们从来不曾、现在也不对帝国感兴趣。诚然，其余各国皆肇始于亚特兰蒂斯的殖民地，但从未有哪一国是违背其选民意愿而被强留在殖民地位的。所有国家过去是、现在是姐妹之邦。我们同荣同辱。亚特兰蒂斯这位母邦，过去是、现在是一个清算中枢、一个协调努力的枢纽，却从未要求或谋求过统治之权；一切决定皆以自由辩论与自由无记名投票为据。
“可如今呢！党派与派系四处丛生，连古老的亚特兰蒂斯也不例外。每个国家都被内讧与争斗撕扯。还不止于此。乌伊加尔（Uighar）举国对南方群岛妒火中烧，南方群岛反过来又嫉妒玛雅（Maya）；玛雅嫉妒班图（Bantu），班图嫉妒埃科普特（Ekopt），埃科普特嫉妒诺海姆（Norheim），诺海姆又嫉妒乌伊加尔。一个恶性的循环，还被纵横交错的其他妒恨越缠越死。每一国都怕别国即将夺取整个世界的控制权；而且一种全无根据的信念似乎正在迅速蔓延：亚特兰蒂斯自己即将把地球上其余各国尽数降为藩属。
“以上便是依我之见对当今世界状况的直白陈述。既然在我们民主政体的既有框架之内，我看不出还有别的路可走，我建议我们继续目前的工作，例如正在推进的各项国际条约与协定，并尽可能加码使劲。下面请政治家克莱托发言。”
“你把局势讲得够清楚了，法罗斯。

===Original===

My thought, however, is that the principal cause of the trouble is the coming into being of this multiplicity of political parties, particularly those composed principally of crackpots and extremists. The connection with atomic energy is clear: since the atomic bomb gives a small group of people the power to destroy the world, they reason that it thereby confers upon them the authority to dictate to the world. My recommendation is merely a special case of yours; that every effort be made to influence the electorates of Norheim and of Uighar into supporting an effective international control of atomic energy.”
“You have your data tabulated in symbolics?” asked Talmonides, from his seat at the keyboard of a calculating machine.
“Yes. Here they are.”
“Thanks.”
“Minister Philamon,” the Faros announced.
“As I see it﻿—as any intelligent man should be able to see it﻿—the principal contribution of atomic energy to this worldwide chaos was the complete demoralization of labor,” the gray-haired Minister of Trade stated, flatly. “Output per man-hour should have gone up at least twenty percent, in which case prices would automatically have come down. Instead, shortsighted guilds imposed drastic curbs on production, and now seem to be surprised that as production falls and hourly wages rise, prices also rise and real income drops. Only one course is possible, gentlemen; labor *must* be made to listen to reason. This featherbedding, this protected loafing, this.﻿ ﻿…”
“I protest!” Marxes, Minister of Work, leaped to his feet. “The blame lies squarely with the capitalists. Their greed, their rapacity, their exploitation of.﻿ ﻿…”
“One moment, please!” Ariponides rapped the table sharply. “It is highly significant of the deplorable condition of the times that two Ministers of State should speak as you two have just spoken. I take it that neither of you has anything new to contribute to this symposium?”
Both claimed the floor, but both were refused it by vote.
“Hand your tabulated data to Talmonides,” the Faros directed. “Officer Artomenes?”
“You, our Faros, have more than intimated that our defense program, for which I am primarily responsible, has been largely to blame for what has happened,” the grizzled warrior began. “In part, perhaps it was﻿—one must be blind indeed not to see the connection, and biased indeed not to admit it.

===Chinese===

不过依我看，祸乱的主因在于这为数众多的政党竟相而生，尤其是那些基本由怪人和极端分子拼成的政党。与原子能的干系一目了然：既然原子弹把毁灭世界的力量交到一小撮人手里，他们便由此推论，这也把对世界发号施令的权力授予了他们。我的建议不过是你那条建议的一个特例：尽力促使诺海姆与乌伊加尔的选民，转而支持对原子能实行有效的国际管制。”
“你的数据用符号编码归档了？”塔尔莫尼德斯坐在计算机键盘前问道。
“归档了。在这儿。”
“多谢。”
“菲拉蒙部长。”法罗斯点名。
“依我看——也是任何明白人理应看出来的——原子能对这场全球乱局的主要‘贡献’，是劳动界的彻底败坏。”白发苍苍的贸易部长平板地说。“人均工时产出本该至少提高两成，那样物价自然会随之回落。结果呢，短视的行会硬给生产套上严苛的枷锁，如今倒好像很惊讶：产量下降而时薪上涨，物价便随之上涨，实际收入反见缩水。诸位，只有一条路可走：必须让劳动界听得进道理。这种虚领干薪、这种受庇护的游手好闲、这种……”
“我抗议！”劳务部长马克塞斯霍地起立。“罪责明明白白全在资本家身上。他们的贪婪、他们的横征暴敛、他们对……的剥削……”
“请稍等！”阿里波尼德斯重重叩响桌面。“两位国务部长竟如你们方才这般讲话，仅此一端，便足见时局的颓败到了何等地步。我是否可以认为，二位对这场研讨再无任何新见可贡献？”
两人都要求发言，却双双被表决驳回。
“把你们归档的数据交给塔尔莫尼德斯。”法罗斯吩咐。“统帅阿尔托梅内斯？”
“您，我们的法罗斯，已经不止是暗示：由我负首责的国防计划，对今日之局难辞其咎。”头发花白的军人开了腔。“部分而言，或许如此——看不见这层关联的，眼睛必是瞎了；不肯承认的，心必是偏了。

===Original===

But what should I have done, knowing that there is no practical defense against the atomic bomb? Every nation has them, and is manufacturing more and more. Every nation is infested with the agents of every other. Should I have tried to keep Atlantis toothless in a world bristling with fangs? And could I﻿—or anyone else﻿—have succeeded in doing so?”
“Probably not. No criticism was intended; we must deal with the situation as it actually exists. Your recommendations, please?”
“I have thought this thing over day and night, and can see no solution which can be made acceptable to our﻿—or to any real﻿—democracy. Nevertheless, I have one recommendation to make. We all know that Norheim and Uighar are the sore spots﻿—particularly Norheim. We have more bombs as of now than both of them together. We know that Uighar’s supersonic jobs are ready. We don’t know exactly what Norheim has, since they cut my Intelligence line a while back, but I’m sending over another operative﻿—my best man, too﻿—tonight. If he finds out that we have enough advantage in speed, and I’m pretty sure that we have, I say hit both Norheim and Uighar right then, while we can, before they hit us. And hit them hard﻿—pulverize them. Then set up a world government strong enough to knock out any nation﻿—including Atlantis﻿—that will not cooperate with it. This course of action is flagrantly against all international law and all the principles of democracy, I know; and even it might not work. It is, however, as far as I can see, the only course which *can* work.”
“You﻿—we all﻿—perceive its weaknesses.” The Faros thought for minutes. “You cannot be sure that your Intelligence has located all of the danger points, and many of them must be so far underground as to be safe from even our heaviest missiles. We all, including you, believe that the Psychologist is right in holding that the reaction of the other nations to such action would be both unfavorable and violent. Your report, please, Talmonides.”
“I have already put my data into the integrator.” The Psychologist punched a button and the mechanism began to whir and to click. “I have only one new fact of any importance; the name of one of the higher-ups and its corollary implication that there may be some degree of cooperation between Norheim and Uighar.﻿ ﻿…”
He broke off as the machine stopped clicking and ejected its report.

===Chinese===

可是，明知原子弹并无任何切实可行的防御之法，我又当如何？国国皆有，而且越造越多。国国境内都遍布他国的特务。难道要我让亚特兰蒂斯在一个獠牙林立的世界里缺了牙口？再者，我——或随便什么人——当真办得到吗？”
“多半办不到。此非责备之意；我们必须依照实际存在的局面来应对。请讲你的建议。”
“此事我日夜苦思，想不出任何能被我们的——或任何真正的——民主政体接受的办法。尽管如此，我有一条建议。我们全都知道，诺海姆与乌伊加尔是两处溃疡——尤其是诺海姆。眼下的炸弹，我们比他们两家加起来还多。我们知道乌伊加尔那些超音速家伙已然就绪。至于诺海姆手里究竟有什么，自前一阵他们掐断我的情报线后便无从知晓；不过今晚我再派一名特务过去——还是我最得力的一个。倘若他查明我们在速度上占有足够的优势——这一点我颇有把握——那我说，趁还来得及，趁他们尚未先动手，即刻同时打击诺海姆与乌伊加尔。狠狠地打——把他们碾成齑粉。然后树立一个足够强大的世界政府，足以击垮任何不肯合作的国度——包括亚特兰蒂斯在内。我知道这一手悍然践踏一切国际法、践踏民主的全部原则；而且连它也未必奏效。然而，依我所见，这是唯一可能奏效的一手。”
“你——我们大家——都看得出它的种种弱点。”法罗斯沉吟了几分钟。“你无法确信你的情报部门已锁定全部危险地点，而其中许多必定深藏地下，连我们最重的导弹也奈何不得。我们所有人，包括你在内，都认为心理学家言之有理：他国对此等行径的反应，必是敌对而暴烈的。请汇报吧，塔尔莫尼德斯。”
“我已把数据送入积分仪。”心理学家按下一枚按钮，机器开始嗡嗡、咔哒作响。“我只有一条要紧的新情报：一个高层人物的名字，及其顺理成章的隐义——诺海姆与乌伊加尔之间，或许存在某种程度的合作……”
机器停止咔哒、吐出报告，他随即收了声。

===Original===

“Look at that graph﻿—up ten points in seven days!” Talmonides pointed a finger. “The situation is deteriorating faster and faster. The conclusion is unavoidable﻿—you can see yourselves that this summation line is fast approaching unity﻿—that the outbreaks will become uncontrollable in approximately eight days. With one slight exception﻿—here﻿—you will notice that the lines of organization and purpose are as random as ever. In spite of this conclusive integration I would be tempted to believe that this seeming lack of coherence was due to insufficient data﻿—that back of this whole movement there is a carefully-set-up and completely-integrated plan﻿—except for the fact that the factions and the nations are so evenly matched. But the data are sufficient. It is shown conclusively that no one of the other nations can possibly win, even by totally destroying Atlantis. They would merely destroy each other and our entire Civilization. According to this forecast, in arriving at which the data furnished by our Officer were prime determinants, that will surely be the outcome unless remedial measures be taken at once. You are of course sure of your facts, Artomenes?”
“I am sure. But you said you had a name, and that it indicated a Norheim-Uighar hookup. What is that name?”
“An old friend of yours.﻿ ﻿…”
“Lo Sung!” The words as spoken were a curse of fury.
“None other. And, unfortunately, there is as yet no course of action indicated which is at all promising of success.”
“Use mine, then!” Artomenes jumped up and banged the table with his fist. “Let me send two flights of rockets over right now that will blow Uigharstoy and Norgrad into radioactive dust and make a thousand square miles around each of them uninhabitable for ten thousand years! If that’s the only way they can learn anything, let them learn!”
“Sit down, Officer,” Ariponides directed, quietly. “That course, as you have already pointed out, is indefensible. It violates every Prime Basic of our Civilization. Moreover, it would be entirely futile, since this resultant makes it clear that every nation on Earth would be destroyed within the day.”
“What, then?” Artomenes demanded, bitterly. “Sit still here and let them annihilate us?”
“Not necessarily. It is to formulate plans that we are here. Talmonides will by now have decided, upon the basis of our pooled knowledge, what must be done.”

===Chinese===

“瞧那条曲线——七天蹿了十点！”塔尔莫尼德斯伸指一点。“局势恶化得越来越快。结论无从回避——诸位自己也看得见，这条总合线正迅速逼近整数一——大约八天之内，暴动将全面失控。只有一处细微的例外——这里——你们会注意到，组织与目标两条曲线依旧杂乱如故。尽管积分结果如此明确，我本要倾向于认为，这种表面上的涣散源于数据不足——这整场运动背后另有一套精心布设、环环相扣的计划——若不是各方各国的势力如此旗鼓相当。但数据是充分的。结论已然确凿：其余任何一国都绝无获胜之望，即便彻底毁灭亚特兰蒂斯亦然。它们只会彼此毁灭，并连同我们的整个文明一并葬送。依照这项预测——统帅所供数据乃是其中首要的决定因子——除非立即采取补救之策，结局必定如此。阿尔托梅内斯，你对你的情报确信无疑吧？”
“确信。不过你说到手了一个名字，还说它指向诺海姆—乌伊加尔的勾结。什么名字？”
“你的一个老朋友……”
“罗松（Lo Sung）！”话一出口，便成了一句狂怒的诅咒。
“正是此人。而不幸的是，眼下尚无任何行动方案可望成功。”
“那就用我的！”阿尔托梅内斯跳将起来，一拳砸在桌上。“让我即刻派两队火箭过去，把乌伊加尔斯托伊（Uigharstoy）和诺格勒（Norgrad）轰成放射性尘埃，叫两城各自周围一千平方英里一万年寸草不生！既然只有这样才能教会他们一点东西，那就让他们学！”
“坐下，统帅。”阿里波尼德斯平静地说。“那条路，你自己已经指明，是站不住的。它把我们文明的最高基本准则条条践踏，一条不落。何况它也全然无用，因为这份合成结果明明白白：不出一日，地球上每一国都将覆灭。”
“那又如何？”阿尔托梅内斯悲愤质问。“就在这儿干坐，任他们把我们斩尽杀绝？”
“未必。我们正是为定计而来的。塔尔莫尼德斯此刻想必已凭我们汇总的知识，拿定了必须去做的事。”

===Original===

“The outlook is not good: not good at all,” the Psychologist announced, gloomily. “The only course of action which carries any promise whatever of success﻿—and its probability is only point one eight﻿—is the one recommended by the Faros, modified slightly to include Artomenes’ suggestion of sending his best operative on the indicated mission. For highest morale, by the way, the Faros should also interview this agent before he sets out. Ordinarily I would not advocate a course of action having so little likelihood of success; but since it is simply a continuation and intensification of what we are already doing, I do not see how we can adopt any other.”
“Are we agreed?” Ariponides asked, after a short silence.
They were agreed. Four of the conferees filed out and a brisk young man strode in. Although he did not look at the Faros his eyes asked questions.
“Reporting for orders, sir.” He saluted the Officer punctiliously.
“At ease, sir.” Artomenes returned the salute. “You were called here for a word from the Faros. Sir, I present Captain Phryges.”
“Not orders, son﻿ ﻿… no.” Ariponides’ right hand rested in greeting upon the captain’s left shoulder, wise old eyes probed deeply into gold-flecked, tawny eyes of youth; the Faros saw, without really noticing, a flaming thatch of red-bronze-auburn hair. “I asked you here to wish you well; not only for myself, but for all our nation and perhaps for our entire race. While everything in my being rebels against an unprovoked and unannounced assault, we may be compelled to choose between our Officer’s plan of campaign and the destruction of Civilization. Since you already know the vital importance of your mission, I need not enlarge upon it. But I want you to know fully, Captain Phryges, that all Atlantis flies with you this night.”
“Th﻿ ﻿… thank you, sir.” Phryges gulped twice to steady his voice. “I’ll do my best, sir.”
And later, in a wingless craft flying toward the airfield, young Phryges broke a long silence. “So *that* is the Faros﻿ ﻿… I like him, Officer﻿ ﻿… I have never seen him close up before﻿ ﻿… there’s something about him.﻿ ﻿… He isn’t like my father, much, but it seems as though I have known him for a thousand years!”
“Hm﻿ ﻿… m﻿ ﻿… m. Peculiar. You two are a lot alike, at that, even though you don’t look anything like each other.﻿ ﻿… Can’t put a finger on exactly what it is, but it’s there.”

===Chinese===

“前景不妙：非常不妙。”心理学家黯然宣布。“唯一还带一丝成功之望的行动方案——其概率也不过零点一八——便是法罗斯所荐的那一条，略加修正，纳入阿尔托梅内斯派遣最得力特务执行既定任务的提议。另外，为着最高昂的士气，法罗斯还宜在此人动身前亲自接见。换在平时，我不会主张一条成功希望如此渺茫的路线；但它不过是现行做法的延续与加力，我看不出我们还能另择何途。”
“诸位都同意？”阿里波尼德斯在短暂沉默后发问。
众皆同意。四位与会者鱼贯而出，一名精干的青年阔步而入。他没有看法罗斯，眼睛却在发问。
“前来领命，长官。”他一丝不苟地向统帅敬礼。
“稍息。”阿尔托梅内斯回礼。“请你来，是要听法罗斯讲几句话。长官，请容我介绍，这位是弗里吉斯上尉（Phryges）。”
“不是命令，孩子……不是。”阿里波尼德斯右手抚上上尉的左肩致意，一双老眼睿智，深深探进那双年轻而金斑闪动的黄褐色眼睛；法罗斯看见——却并未真个留意——一头红铜掺赤褐的火焰般乱发。“我请你来，是为你送行；不只是为我个人，而是为我们整个国家，或许还为我们整个种族。尽管我本性中的一切都在反抗一场无端不宣的攻击，我们却可能不得不在统帅的作战方案与文明的毁灭之间作一取舍。你既已明白此行使命干系之重，我便无须再赘言。但我愿你知晓无遗，弗里吉斯上尉：今夜，整个亚特兰蒂斯与你一同飞行。”
“谢……谢谢您，长官。”弗里吉斯两度吞咽，才稳住嗓音。“我必竭尽全力，长官。”
随后，在飞往机场的无翼飞行器里，年轻的弗里吉斯打破了长久的沉默。“原来法罗斯是这副样子……我喜欢他，长官……我从未在近处见过他……他身上有种东西……他不大像我父亲，可我竟觉得已认识了他一千年！”
“嗯……嗯……嗯。真是怪事。你们俩倒真有几分相像，虽说长相毫不搭界。……说不上究竟是啥，可那东西确确实实在。”

===Original===

Although Artomenes nor any other of his time could place it, the resemblance was indeed there. It was in and back of the eyes; it was the “look of eagles” which was long later to become associated with the wearers of Arisia’s Lens. “But here we are, and your ship’s ready. Luck, son.”
“Thanks, sir. But one more thing. If it should﻿—if I don’t get back﻿—will you see that my wife and the baby are﻿ ﻿… ?”
“I will, son. They will leave for North Maya tomorrow morning. They will live, whether you and I do or not. Anything else?”
“No, sir. Thanks. Goodbye.”
The ship was a tremendous flying wing. A standard commercial job. Empty﻿—passengers, even crewmen, were never subjected to the brutal accelerations regularly used by unmanned carriers. Phryges scanned the panel. Tiny motors were pulling tapes through the controllers. Every light showed green. Everything was set. Donning a waterproof coverall, he slid through a flexible valve into his acceleration-tank and waited.
A siren yelled briefly. Black night turned blinding white as the harnessed energies of the atom were released. For five and six-tenths seconds the sharp, hard, beryllium-bronze leading edge of the back-sweeping V sliced its way through ever-thinning air.
The vessel seemed to pause momentarily; paused and bucked viciously. She shuddered and shivered, tried to tear herself into shreds and chunks; but Phryges in his tank was unconcerned. Earlier, weaker ships went to pieces against the solid-seeming wall of atmospheric incompressibility at the velocity of sound; but this one was built solidly enough, and powered to hit that wall hard enough, to go through unharmed.
The hellish vibration ceased; the fantastic violence of the drive subsided to a mere shove; Phryges knew that the vessel had leveled off at its cruising speed of two thousand miles per hour. He emerged, spilling the least possible amount of water upon the polished steel floor. He took off his coverall and stuffed it back through the valve into the tank. He mopped and polished the floor with towels, which likewise went into the tank.
He drew on a pair of soft gloves and, by manual control, jettisoned the acceleration tank and all the apparatus which had made that unloading possible. This junk would fall into the ocean; would sink; would never be found. He examined the compartment and the hatch minutely.

===Chinese===

阿尔托梅内斯，连同他那时代的任何人，虽说不出个所以然，这相似却确凿存在。它在眼睛里，在眼睛后面；那是“鹰隼般的目光”，很久以后，它将同佩戴阿里西亚透镜的人联系在一起。“好了，到了，你的船已备妥。走运，孩子。”
“谢谢，长官。不过还有一事。万一——万一我回不来——请您照看我的妻子和孩子，可好……？”
“我会的，孩子。他们明晨启程去北玛雅。不论你我存殁，他们会活下去的。还有别的吗？”
“没有了，长官。谢谢。再见。”
那艘船是一只巨大的飞翼，一架标准型商用机。空的——乘客、乃至机组，从来不必承受无人驾驶运输机习以为常的那种野蛮加速度。弗里吉斯扫视操控台。微型马达正牵着磁带穿过控制器。每盏灯都泛着绿光。一切就绪。他穿上防水连体服，从一道柔性阀门口钻进自己的加速度水槽，静候时刻到来。
汽笛短啸一声。受缚的原子能轰然释放，沉沉黑夜霎时化作炫目白昼。五点六秒钟内，后掠V形机体那薄硬锋锐的铍青铜前缘，一路切开愈发稀薄的大气。
飞行器似有瞬息的停滞；一顿，随即凶猛地颠簸。她颤抖、战栗，像要把自己撕成碎块齑粉；水槽中的弗里吉斯却安之若素。早年间更脆弱的飞机，撞上音速那堵貌似坚实的空气不可压缩之墙，便粉身碎骨；而这一架造得足够结实，动力足以狠狠撞穿那堵墙而全身而退。
地狱般的震动止息；驱动那骇人的暴烈化作一记寻常的推压；弗里吉斯知道，机体已改平，进入每小时两千英里的巡航。他钻出水槽，尽可能少把水洒在抛光的钢地板上。他脱下连体服，从阀门塞回槽内。他用毛巾把地板抹净擦亮，毛巾也一并入槽。
他戴上一副软手套，手动抛掉了加速度水槽，以及令那次卸载成为可能的全套装置。这堆废物将坠入大洋；将沉底；将永无重见天日之期。他把隔舱与舱门里里外外细查了一遍。

===Original===

No scratches, no scars, no mars; no telltale marks or prints of any kind. Let the Norskies search. So far, so good.
Back toward the trailing edge then, to a small escape-hatch beside which was fastened a dull black ball. The anchoring devices went out first. He gasped as the air rushed out into near-vacuum, but he had been trained to take sudden and violent fluctuations in pressure. He rolled the ball out upon the hatch, where he opened it; two hinged hemispheres, each heavily padded with molded composition resembling sponge rubber. It seemed incredible that a man as big as Phryges, especially when wearing a parachute, could be crammed into a space so small; but that lining had been molded to fit.
This ball *had* to be small. The ship, even though it was on a regularly-scheduled commercial flight, would be scanned intensively and continuously from the moment of entering Norheiman radar range. Since the ball would be invisible on any radar screen, no suspicion would be aroused; particularly since﻿—as far as Atlantean Intelligence had been able to discover﻿—the Norheimans had not yet succeeded in perfecting any device by the use of which a living man could bail out of a supersonic plane.
Phryges waited﻿—and waited﻿—until the second hand of his watch marked the arrival of zero time. He curled up into one half of the ball; the other half closed over him and locked. The hatch opened. Ball and closely-prisoned man plummeted downward; slowing abruptly, with a horrible deceleration, to terminal velocity. Had the air been any trifle thicker the Atlantean captain would have died then and there; but that, too, had been computed accurately and Phryges lived.
And as the ball bulleted downward on a screaming slant, it *shrank*!
This, too, the Atlanteans hoped, was new﻿—a synthetic which air-friction would erode away, molecule by molecule, so rapidly that no perceptible fragment of it would reach ground.
The casing disappeared, and the yielding porous lining. And Phryges, still at an altitude of over thirty thousand feet, kicked away the remaining fragments of his cocoon and, by judicious planning, turned himself so that he could see the ground, now dimly visible in the first dull gray of dawn. There was the highway, paralleling his line of flight; he wouldn’t miss it more than a hundred yards.
He fought down an almost overwhelming urge to pull his ripcord too soon.

===Chinese===

没有划痕，没有擦伤，没有疵点；没有任何泄密的可疑痕迹或指纹。让诺斯克佬搜去。到目前为止，一切顺遂。
然后折向机尾，到一扇小小的应急舱门旁；门边固定着一只暗黑的圆球。锚定装置先行抛出。空气猛地涌向近真空，他呛了一口气，但他受过训练，扛得住压力的骤然剧变。他把球滚上舱门，随即开启：两个铰接的半球，内里厚厚衬着模压成型、状似海绵橡胶的复合材料。像弗里吉斯这样的大块头，尤其还背着降落伞，竟能塞进如此狭小的空间，简直匪夷所思；可那层衬里，本就是照他的身形模压的。
这颗球非小不可。这架飞机虽属定期商用航班，可自进入诺海姆雷达范围的一刻起，便会遭到不间断的密集扫掠。这颗球既在任何雷达屏上无影无形，便不会惹起疑心；更何况——就亚特兰蒂斯情报部门所能探知的——诺海姆人尚未造出任何能让活人从超音速飞机上跳伞脱身的装置。
弗里吉斯等着——一等再等——直到表盘上秒针标出零时的到来。他蜷进球的半个壳里；另半个壳覆下，锁死。舱门开启。圆球连同被紧紧禁锢的人急坠而下；随即以骇人的减速度骤然一缓，落到终端速度。空气若再浓上一丝半缕，这位亚特兰蒂斯上尉便当场殒命；可这一点同样被算得精准，弗里吉斯活了下来。
而那颗球循着凄厉的斜线向下疾坠时，它竟在收缩！
这也是亚特兰蒂斯人期望中的新花样——一种合成材料，空气摩擦会将它逐分子蚀尽，快到不会有一片可觉察的碎屑落到地面。
外壳消逝了，随之是那层柔韧多孔的衬里。弗里吉斯尚在三万英尺开外的高空，蹬脱茧壳的残片，又凭精心的算计拧转身躯，好望见地面——黎明最初那层暗灰里，它已隐约可辨。公路就在那里，与他的航线平行；他偏离它不会超过一百码。
他强自按捺住那股几乎压不住的、想过早拉开开伞索的冲动。

===Original===

He had to wait﻿—wait until the last possible second﻿—because parachutes were big and Norheiman radar practically swept the ground.
Low enough at last, he pulled the ring. *Z‑r‑r‑e‑e‑k﻿—**whap**!* The chute banged open; his harness tightened with a savage jerk, mere seconds before his hard-sprung knees took the shock of landing.
