## XIII The Hill / XIII 希尔
===Original===

The heavy cruiser Chicago hung motionless in space, thousands of miles distant from the warring fleets of spaceships so viciously attacking and so stubbornly defending Roger’s planetoid. In the captain’s sanctum Lyman Cleveland crouched tensely above his ultracameras, his sensitive fingers touching lightly their micrometric dials. His body was rigid, his face was set and drawn. Only his eyes moved; flashing back and forth between his instruments and the smoothly-running strands of spring-steel wire upon which were being recorded the frightful scenes of carnage and destruction.
Silent and bitterly absorbed, though surrounded by staring officers whose fervent, almost unconscious cursing was prayerful in its intensity, the visiray expert kept his ultra-instruments upon that awful struggle to its dire conclusion. Flawlessly those instruments noted every detail of the destruction of Roger’s fleet, of the transformation of the armada of Triplanetary into an unknown fluid, and finally of the dissolution of the gigantic planetoid itself. Then furiously Cleveland drove his beam against the crimsonly opaque obscurity into which the peculiar, viscous stream of substance was disappearing. Time after time he applied his every watt of power, with no result. A vast volume of space, roughly ellipsoidal in shape, was closed to him by forces entirely beyond his experience or comprehension. But suddenly, while his rays were still trying to pierce that impenetrable murk, it disappeared instantly and without warning: the illimitable infinity of space once more lay revealed upon his plates and his beams flashed unimpeded through the void.
“Back to Tellus, sir?” The *Chicago*’s captain broke the strained silence.
“I wouldn’t say so, if I had the say.” Cleveland, baffled and frustrated, straightened up and shut off his cameras. “We should report back as soon as possible, of course, but there seems to be a lot of wreckage out there yet that we can’t photograph in detail at this distance. A close study of it might help us a lot in understanding what they did and how they did it. I’d say that we should get closeups of whatever is left, and do it right away, before it gets scattered all over space; but of course I can’t give you orders.”
“You can, though,” the captain made surprising answer. “My orders are that you are in command of this vessel.”

===Chinese===

重型巡洋舰芝加哥号悬停在太空中，一动不动，与那支凶猛进攻、殊死防守罗杰小行星的太空舰队相距数千英里。在船长专用舱里，莱曼·克利夫兰（Lyman Cleveland）俯身紧盯着他的超摄影机，敏感的手指轻轻搭在微调度盘上。他身体僵直，面容绷紧。只有眼睛在动；在仪器与那几股平稳运行的弹簧钢丝之间来回扫视——钢丝上正记录着屠杀与毁灭的可怖景象。
四周尽是瞪圆了眼的军官，他们那近乎不自觉的切齿咒骂热烈得如同祈祷；这位视射线（visiray）专家却沉默而痛苦地专注着，把他的超波（ultra-wave）仪器死死对准那场恶战，直到它惨烈的终局。那些仪器无瑕地记下了每一个细节：罗杰舰队的覆灭、三行星（Triplanetary）大军化作某种未知流体、以及最后那颗巨大小行星自身的消解。随后克利夫兰狂怒地驱动光束，顶向那片深红不透明的迷障——那股奇异而黏稠的物质流正消失其中。他一次又一次倾尽每一瓦功率，毫无结果。一片广袤的空间，大体呈椭球形，被某种全然超出他经验与理解的力量对他封锁了。可是突然，在他的射线仍在试图刺穿那片不可透入的黑暗时，它霎时消失，毫无征兆：无垠的太空重新铺展在他的底板上，他的光束在虚空中畅行无阻。
“回特卢斯星吗，长官？”芝加哥号的船长打破了紧绷的沉默。
“我要是有发言权，就不回去。”克利夫兰又挫又恼，直起身来，关掉摄影机。“尽快汇报当然是应该的，可那边似乎还散着大量残骸，隔这么远没法拍出细节。凑近研究一下，说不定大有裨益，能帮我们弄明白他们干了什么、又是怎么干的。依我说，凡是剩下的都该拍一套特写，而且马上就拍，趁残骸还没散遍太空；不过我当然无权向您下令。”
“你有权。”船长的回答出人意料。“我接到的命令是：本舰由您指挥。”

===Original===

“In that case we will proceed at full emergency acceleration to investigate the wreckage,” Cleveland replied, and the cruiser﻿—sole survivor of Triplanetary’s supposedly invincible force﻿—shot away with every projector delivering its maximum blast.
As the scene of the disaster was approached there was revealed upon the plates a confused mass of debris; a mass whose individual units were apparently moving at random, yet which was as a whole still following the orbit of Roger’s planetoid. Space was full of machine parts, structural members, furniture, flotsam of all kinds; and everywhere were the bodies of men. Some were encased in spacesuits, and it was to these that the rescuers turned first﻿—space-hardened veterans though the men of the *Chicago* were, they did not care even to look at the others. Strangely enough, however, not one of the floating figures spoke or moved, and space-line men were hurriedly sent out to investigate.
“All dead.” Quickly the dread report came back. “Been dead a long time. The armor is all stripped off the suits, and all the generators and other apparatus are all shot. Something funny about it, too﻿—none of them seem to have been touched, but the machinery of the suits seems to be about half missing.”
“I’ve got it all on the reels, sir.” Cleveland, his closeup survey of the wreckage finished, turned to the captain. “What they’ve just reported checks up with what I have photographed everywhere. I’ve got an idea of what might have happened, but it’s so new that I’ll have to have some evidence before I’ll believe it myself. You might have them bring in a few of the armored bodies, a couple of those switchboards and panels floating around out there, and half a dozen miscellaneous pieces of junk﻿—the nearest things they get hold of, whatever they happen to be.”
“Then back to Tellus at maximum?”
“Right﻿—back to Tellus, as fast as we can possibly get there.”
While the *Chicago* hurtled through space at full power, Cleveland and the ranking officers of the vessel grouped themselves about the salvaged wreckage. Familiar with space-wrecks as were they all, none of them had ever seen anything like the material before them. For every part and instrument was weirdly and meaninglessly disintegrated. There were no breaks, no marks of violence, and yet nothing was intact.

===Chinese===

“这样的话，我们就以全紧急加速度前进，去勘查残骸。”克利夫兰答道。于是这艘巡洋舰——三行星那支号称不可战胜的舰队唯一的幸存者——全数投射器以最大威力开火，疾射而去。
逼近灾难现场时，底板上显出一大团纷乱的碎片；碎块各自看似乱窜，整体却仍循着罗杰小行星的轨道。太空中满是机器零件、结构件、家具、各色漂浮物；处处是人的尸体。有的裹在太空服里，救援者便先奔这些人去——芝加哥号上的人虽都是太空铁汉，其余那些尸体他们连看都不忍看。然而蹊跷的是，漂浮的人形没有一个开口或动弹，太空索作业手被急忙放出去查探。
“全死了。”可怖的报告很快传回。“死了很久了。太空服上的装甲全被剥光，所有发电机和其他设备全被打穿。还有桩怪事——人身上似乎一个伤都没有，可太空服的机械装置倒缺了约莫一半。”
“我都收进卷片里了，长官。”克利夫兰对残骸的近距扫描已毕，转向船长。“他们方才报告的，与我到处拍到的情况对得上。出了什么事，我心里有个想法，只是它太离奇，我自己也得先拿到证据才敢信。您可以叫他们收几具装甲尸身进来，再捞一两块在外面漂的交换机和操控板，外加六七件零碎破烂——手边够着什么就捞什么，不论是什么。”
“然后全速回特卢斯星？”
“正是——回特卢斯星，多快赶多快。”
芝加哥号全功率在太空中疾驰，克利夫兰与舰上高级军官围着打捞的残骸。他们个个见惯了太空海难，却谁也没见过眼前的材料。因为每一个零件、每一台仪器，都离奇而无意义地崩解了。没有裂口，没有暴力痕迹，却又无一完好。

===Original===

Bolt-holes stared empty, cores, shielding cases and needles had disappeared, the vital parts of every instrument hung awry, disorganization reigned rampant and supreme.
“I never imagined such a mess,” the captain said, after a long and silent study of the objects. “If you have a theory to cover *that*, Cleveland, I would like to hear it!”
“I want you to notice something first,” the expert replied. “But don’t look for what’s there﻿—look for what *isn’t* there.”
“Well, the armor is gone. So are the shielding cases, shafts, spindles, the housings and stems﻿ ﻿…” the captain’s voice died away as his eyes raced over the collection. “Why everything that was made of wood, bakelite, copper, aluminum, silver, bronze, or anything but steel hasn’t been touched, and every bit of that is gone. But that doesn’t make sense﻿—what does it mean?”
“I don’t know﻿—yet,” Cleveland replied, slowly. “But I’m afraid that there’s more, and worse.” He opened a spacesuit reverently, revealing the face; a face calm and peaceful, but utterly, sickeningly white. Still reverently, he made a deep incision in the brawny neck, severing the jugular vein, then went on, soberly:
“You never imagined such a thing as *white* blood, either, but it all checks up. Someway, somehow, every atom of free or combined iron in this whole volume of space was made off with.”
“Huh? How come? And above all, *why*?” from the amazed and staring officers.
“You know as much as I do,” grimly, ponderingly. “If it were not for the fact that there are solid asteroids of iron out beyond Mars, I would say that somebody wanted iron badly enough to wipe out the fleet and the planetoid to get it. But anyway, whoever they were, they carried enough power so that our armament didn’t bother them at all. They simply took the metal they wanted and went away with it﻿—so fast that I couldn’t trace them with an ultra-beam. There’s only one thing plain; but that’s so plain that it scares me stiff. This whole affair spells intelligence, with a capital *I*, and that intelligence is anything but friendly. I want to put Fred Rodebush at work on this just as fast as I can get him.”
He stepped over to his ultra-projector and put in a call for Virgil Samms, whose face soon appeared upon his screen.
“We got it all, Virgil,” he reported. “It’s something extraordinary﻿—bigger, wider, and deeper than any of us dreamed.

===Chinese===

螺栓孔空空地张着，磁芯、屏蔽壳与指针尽数消失，每台仪器的要害部件歪斜悬荡，涣散与错乱君临一切。
“我从未想到会有这么一团糟。”船长对那些物件默默端详良久后才说。“你若有理论能解释这个，克利夫兰，我倒要听听！”
“我要你先注意一样东西。”专家答道。“但别找还在的——找不在的。”
“唔，装甲没了。屏蔽壳、轴、心轴、套壳和杆柄也都没了……”船长的声音低下去，眼睛在那一堆东西上飞快逡巡。“怪了，凡是木头、电木、铜、铝、银、青铜做的，总之除钢以外的一切，都原封未动；凡是钢，一丁点不剩。可这讲不通啊——什么意思？”
“我还不知道——暂时。”克利夫兰缓缓答道。“可我怕还有更糟的。”他恭谨地打开一套太空服，露出那张脸；那张脸安详平静，却白得彻头彻尾、白得令人作呕。他依旧恭谨地在死者粗壮的颈上深切一刀，割断颈静脉，而后沉声说：
“白色的血，这样的东西你也从未想过吧，可一切都能对上。不知怎么、不知何故，这一整片空间里，无论游离的还是化合的铁，每一个原子都被人取走了。”
“啊？怎么会？更要紧的是，为什么？”愕然瞪视的军官们问。
“你知道的和我一样多。”克利夫兰面色阴沉，若有所思。“要不是火星外头还有成座的固态铁质小行星，我真要说，有谁急需铁，急到肯把整支舰队连同小行星一并抹掉来取它。但无论如何，不管他们是谁，他们携的功率大到我们的武备根本奈何他们不得。他们径直取走想要的金属，扬长而去——快到我用超波都追不出踪迹。只有一件事明摆着；明摆到叫我脊背发凉。这整桩事，拼出来的是智力，字头大写的智力；而那智力，绝谈不上友善。我要尽快把弗雷德·罗德布什抓来做这件事。”
他跨到超波投射器前，呼叫弗吉尔·萨姆斯（Virgil Samms）；那张脸很快出现在他的护屏（screens）上。
“我们全拍下来了，弗吉尔。”他汇报道。“这事情非同小可——比我们谁做梦梦到的都更大、更宽、更深。

===Original===

It may be urgent, too, so I think I had better shoot the stuff in on an ultra-beam and save some time. Fred has a telemagneto recorder there that he can synchronize with this outfit easily enough. Right?”
“Right. Good work, Lyman﻿—thanks,” came back terse approval and appreciation, and soon the steel wires were again flashing from reel to reel. This time, however, their varying magnetic charges were so modulating ultra-waves that every detail of that calamitous battle of the void was being screened and recorded in the innermost private laboratory of the Triplanetary Service.
Eager though he naturally was to join his fellow-scientists, Cleveland was not impatient during the long, but uneventful journey back to Earth. There was much to study, many improvements to be made in his comparatively crude first ultra-camera. Then, too, there were long conferences with Samms, and particularly with Rodebush, the nuclear physicist, who would have to do much of the work involved in solving the riddles of the energies and weapons of the Nevians. Thus it did not seem long before green Terra grew large beneath the flying sphere of the *Chicago*.
“Going to have to circle it once, aren’t you?” Cleveland asked the chief pilot. He had been watching that officer closely for minutes, admiring the delicacy and precision with which the great vessel was being maneuvered preliminary to entering the Earth’s atmosphere.
“Yes,” the pilot replied. “We had to come in in the shortest possible time, and that meant a velocity here that we can’t check without a spiral. However, even at that we saved a lot of time. You can save quite a bit more, though, by having a rocket-plane come out to meet us somewhere around fifteen or twenty thousand kilometers, depending upon where you want to land. With their drives they can match our velocity and still make the drop direct.”
“Guess I’ll do that﻿—thanks,” and the operative called his chief, only to learn that his suggestion had already been acted upon.
“We beat you to it, Lyman,” Samms smiled. “The *Silver Sliver* is out there now, looping to match your course, acceleraction, and velocity at twenty two thousand kilometers. You’ll be ready to transfer?”
“I’ll be ready,” and the Quartermaster’s ex-clerk went to his quarters and packed his dunnage-bag.

===Chinese===

说不定还十万火急，所以我想最好用超波把材料直接传回去，省些时间。弗雷德那边有台遥磁记录仪，跟这套设备同步起来不难。行吗？”
“行。干得好，莱曼——多谢。”回复简短，嘉许与谢意俱全；很快，钢质导线又从一盘飞转到另一盘。只是这一回，它们变化的磁荷正调制着超波，那场太空浩劫的每一个细节，正被播送并记录到三行星服务团最机密的内室里。
克利夫兰自然急欲与同僚科学家会合，但在回地球漫长而平静的航程中倒也不觉难熬。要研究的东西很多，他那台相对粗糙的第一代超摄影机也大有可改进之处。再者，还有与萨姆斯、尤其是与罗德布什——那位核物理学家——的长谈；内维亚人的能量与兵器之谜，大半要靠他去解。如此一来，不等觉得路长，绿色的泰拉已在芝加哥号的飞球下方越变越大。
“还得绕它一圈，是吧？”克利夫兰问主驾驶。几分钟来他一直细看那位军官，钦佩这艘巨舰入大气前的机动如此轻灵精准。
“是。”驾驶员答。“我们必须以最短时间抵达，这就意味着此地的速度非盘螺旋不能刹住。不过饶是如此也省了不少时间。您还能再省一截：叫一架火箭飞机出来接我们，会合点放在一万五到两万公里之间，看您想降在哪儿。凭他们的推进力，既咬得住我们的速度，还能直接下坠。”
“我看就这么办——多谢。”这位干员呼叫上级，却得知他的建议早已有人照办。
“抢在你前头了，莱曼。”萨姆斯笑道。“银签号已经在外面，正盘旋匹配你们的航线、加速度和速度，高度两万两千公里。您这就准备换乘？”
“随时准备。”这位军需官从前的书记员回舱收拾行囊去了。

===Original===

In due time the long, slender body of the rocket-plane came into view, creeping “down” upon the spaceship from “above,” and Cleveland bade his friends goodbye. Donning a spacesuit, he stationed himself in the starboard airlock. Its atmosphere was withdrawn, the outer door opened, and he glanced across a bare hundred feet of space at the rocket-plane which, keel ports fiercely aflame, was braking her terrific speed to match the slower pace of the gigantic sphere of war. Shaped like a toothpick, needle-pointed fore and aft, with ultra-stubby wings and vanes, with flush-set rocket ports everywhere, built of a lustrous, silvery alloy of noble and almost infusible metals﻿—such was the private speedboat of Triplanetary’s head man. The fastest thing known, whether in planetary air, the stratosphere, or the vacuous depth of interplanetary space, her first flashing trial spins had won her the nickname of the *Silver Sliver*. She had had a more formal name, but that title had long since been buried in the Departmental files.
Lower and lower dropped the speedboat, her rockets flaming ever brighter, until her slender length lay level with the airlock door. Then her blasting discharges subsided to the power necessary to match exactly the *Chicago*’s acceleration.
“Ready to cut, *Chicago*! Give me a three-second call!” snapped from the pilot room of the *Sliver*.
“Ready to cut!” the pilot of the *Chicago* replied. “Seconds! Three! Two! One! **Cut!**”
At the last word the power of both vessels was instantly cut off and everything in them became weightless. In the tiny airlock of the slender plane crouched a space-line man with coiled cable in readiness, but he was not needed. As the flaring exhausts ceased Cleveland swung out his heavy bag and stepped lightly off into space, and in a right line he floated directly into the open port of the rocket-plane. The door clanged shut behind him and in a matter of moments he stood in the control room of the racer, divested of his armor and shaking hands with his friend and co-laborer, Frederick Rodebush.
“Well, Fritz, what do you know?” Cleveland asked, as soon as greetings had been exchanged. “How do the various reports dovetail together? I know that you couldn’t tell me anything on the wave, but there’s no danger of eavesdroppers *here*.”
“You can’t tell,” Rodebush soberly replied.

===Chinese===

到时辰，火箭飞机修长的机身映入眼帘，从“上方”朝飞船徐徐“降落”下来；克利夫兰与朋友道别。他穿上太空服，在右舷气闸就位。闸内空气抽尽，外门开启，他隔着区区一百英尺的空隙望向那架火箭飞机——龙骨喷口烈焰狂喷，正刹住它骇人的速度，去咬合那艘战争巨球的较慢步子。形如牙签，首尾皆针锋，翼与舵短得出奇，周身密嵌齐平的火箭喷口，通体用一种闪亮的银色合金铸成——高贵而几难熔化的金属——这便是三行星首脑的私人快艇。无论在行星大气、平流层，还是行星际空间的虚空深处，它都是已知最快的东西；头几次炫目的试飞，便为它赢得了“银签号”的外号。它本有个正式名字，只是那名号早埋进部门的档案堆里了。
快艇越降越低，火箭喷出的光焰越燃越亮，直到它细长的机身与气闸门齐平。随后它的轰射减弱到恰好贴死芝加哥号加速度的功率。
“准备切车，芝加哥号！给我三秒倒数！”银签号驾驶室里传来短促的呼喝。
“准备切车！”芝加哥号的驾驶员应声。“倒数！三！二！一！**切！**”
末字出口，两船的动力霎时切断，舱内一切失重。细长飞机那小小的气闸里，蹲着一名备好盘缆的太空索作业手，但用不上他。喷焰一熄，克利夫兰抡起沉重的行囊，轻轻一步跨进太空，沿一条直线径直飘进火箭飞机敞开的舱口。舱门在他身后哐当关死；不多时他已站在赛艇的操纵室里，卸去甲胄，与好友兼同事弗雷德里克·罗德布什握手。
“怎么样，弗里茨，你都知道些什么？”寒暄一过克利夫兰便问。“各路报告对得拢吗？我知道你在波道上什么也不能说，可这儿总不怕人窃听了。”
“不好说。”罗德布什正色答道。

===Original===

“We’re just beginning to wake up to the fact that there are a lot of things we don’t know anything about. Better wait until we’re back at the Hill. We have a full set of ultra screens around there now. There’s a couple of other good reasons, too﻿—it would be better for both of us to go over the whole thing with Virgil, from the ground up; and we can’t do any more talking, anyway. Our orders are to get back there at maximum, and you know what that means aboard the *Sliver*. Strap yourself solid in that shock-absorber there, and here’s a pair of earplugs.”
“When the *Sliver* really cuts loose it means a rough party, all right,” Cleveland assented, snapping about his body the heavy spring-straps of his deeply cushioned seat, “but I’m just as anxious to get back to the Hill as anybody can be to get me there. All set.”
Rodebush waved his hand at the pilot and the purring whisper of the exhausts changed instantly to a deafening, continuous explosion. The men were pressed deeply into their shock-absorbing chairs as the *Silver Sliver* spun around her longitudinal axis and darted away from the *Chicago* with such a tremendous acceleration that the spherical warship seemed to be standing still in space. In due time the calculated midpoint was reached, the slim space-plane rolled over again, and, mad acceleration now reversed, rushed on toward the Earth, but with constantly diminishing speed. Finally a measurable atmospheric pressure was encountered, the needle prow dipped downward, and the *Silver Sliver* shot forward upon her tiny wings and vanes, nose-rockets now drumming in staccato thunder. Her metal grew hot; dull red, bright red, yellow, blinding white; but it neither melted nor burned. The pilot’s calculations had been sound, and though the limiting point of safety of temperature was reached and steadily held, it was not exceeded. As the density of the air increased so decreased the velocity of the man-made meteorite. So it was that a dazzling lance of fire sped high over Seattle, lower over Spokane, and hurled itself eastward, a furiously flaming arrow; slanting downward in a long, screaming dive toward the heart of the Rockies.

===Chinese===

“我们才刚开始醒悟：世上多的是我们一无所知的东西。还是等回了希尔（the Hill）再说。那边如今围了一整套超波护屏。还有两条别的理由——这件事最好我们俩同弗吉尔从头至尾一同过一遍；再说反正也谈不成了。命令是全速赶回去，银签号上的全速是什么滋味，你是知道的。把自己在那张减震椅上捆瓷实，喏，再给你一副耳塞。”
“银签号真撒起野来，确实是场硬仗。”克利夫兰表示同意，一边把深深衬垫的座椅上粗重的弹簧绑带啪啪扣上身，“可天下没有谁比我更急着回希尔了。一切就绪。”
罗德布什朝驾驶员一挥手，排气口低吟的絮语霎时化作震耳欲聋的连绵爆响。银签号绕自身纵轴一旋，以骇人的加速度自芝加哥号旁电射而去，两名汉子被深深压进减震椅，那艘球形战舰仿佛在太空中原地不动了。到时辰，算定的中点已过，细长的太空机又是一滚，疯狂的反向加速度推着它扑向地球，速度却在不断削减。终于，测得出大气压了，针一般的船首向下俯冲，银签号撑开小小的翼与舵前掠，头部火箭此刻擂出断续的雷鸣。艇身金属烧得灼热；暗红、亮红、黄、炫白；却不熔不燃。驾驶员的算计滴水不漏，温度的安全极限一点即至、稳稳守住，却未逾越半分。空气越稠，这枚人造陨星的速度越低。于是一道炫目的火枪高高掠过西雅图，低了些掠过斯波坎，随即向东猛掷，一支狂燃的火箭；长长的、嘶啸的俯冲，斜插落基山的心脏。

===Original===

As the now rapidly cooling greyhound of the skies passed over the western ranges of the Bitter Roots it became apparent that her goal was a vast, flat-topped, conical mountain, shrouded in violet light; a mountain whose height awed even its stupendous neighbors.
While not artificial, the Hill had been altered markedly by the engineers who had built into it the headquarters of the Triplanetary Service. Its mile-wide top was a jointless expanse of gray armor steel; the steep, smooth surface of the truncated cone was a continuation of the same immensely thick sheet of metal. No known vehicle could climb that smooth, hard, forbidding slope of steel; no known projectile could mar that armor; no known craft could even approach the Hill without detection. Could not approach it at all, in fact, for it was constantly enclosed in a vast hemisphere of lambent violet flame through which neither material substance nor destructive ray could pass.
As the *Silver Sliver*, crawling along at a bare five hundred miles an hour, approached that transparent, brilliantly violet wall of destruction, a light of the same color filled her control room and as suddenly went out; flashing on and off again and again.
“Giving us the once-over, eh?” Cleveland asked. “That’s something new, isn’t it?”
“Yes, it’s a high-powered ultra-wave spy,” Rodebush returned. “The light is simply a warning, which can be carried if desired. It can also carry voice and vision.﻿ ﻿…”
“Like this,” Samms’ voice interrupted from a speaker upon the pilot’s panel and his clear-cut face appeared upon the television screen. “I don’t suppose Fred thought to mention it, but this is one of his inventions of the last few days. We are just trying it out on you. It doesn’t mean a thing though, as far as the *Sliver* is concerned. Come ahead!”
A circular opening appeared on the wall of force, an opening which disappeared as soon as the plane had darted through it; and at the same time her landing-cradle rose into the air through a great trapdoor. Slowly and gracefully the space-plane settled downward into that cushioned embrace. Then cradle and nestled *Sliver* sank from view and, turning smoothly upon mighty trunnions, the plug of armor drove solidly back into its place in the metal pavement of the mountain’s lofty summit.

===Chinese===

当这架正急速冷却的天空猎犬飞越比特鲁特山脉西麓诸峰时，它的去向已一目了然：一座浩大、平顶、圆锥形的高山，笼在紫罗兰色的光里；那山的高度，连它周围惊天的巨峰也要仰之肃然。
希尔虽非人工所筑，却已被改建它的工程师们大加改造，把三行星服务团的总部筑进了山体。山顶宽达一英里，是一整片无缝的灰色装甲钢；截顶锥陡峭光洁的坡面，是同一张极厚钢板的延续。没有一种已知的载具爬得上那面光滑、坚硬、冷峻的钢坡；没有一种已知的射弹损伤得了那身装甲；没有一种已知的飞行器能躲过探测接近希尔。事实上根本接近不了，因为它终年裹在一座庞大的、光焰摇曳的紫色半球里，无论实体还是毁灭性射线，皆透它不过。
银签号以区区每小时五百英里匍匐前行，逼近那道透明而紫光灼灼的毁灭之墙时，一束同色的光灌满它的操纵室，又倏然而灭；一明一灭，闪了又闪。
“给我们过筛子呢，呃？”克利夫兰问。“这可是新鲜玩意儿吧？”
“是，一台大功率超波侦察器。”罗德布什答。“那光只是个警示，愿意的话也可以带上。它还能传声传像……”
“就像这样。”萨姆斯的声音从驾驶员面板的一只喇叭里插进来，他轮廓分明的脸出现在电视屏上。“我料想弗雷德没想到提它；这是他最近几天的一项发明。我们正拿你试呢。不过就银签号而言，这不碍任何事。进来吧！”
力墙上绽开一个圆洞，飞机一穿而过，洞口随即弥合；与此同时，它的落地托架透过一扇巨大的活板门升入空中。太空机从容而优雅地沉降进那衬垫的怀抱。随后托架与安卧其上的银签号一同沉没不见；装甲巨塞在强大的耳轴上平滑转动，严丝合缝地旋回山顶金属地面里自己的原位。

===Original===

The cradle-elevator dropped rapidly, coming to rest many levels down in the heart of the Hill, and Cleveland and Rodebush leaped lightly out of their transport, through her still hot outer walls. A door opened before them and they found themselves in a large room of unshadowed daylight illumination; the office of the Chief of the Triplanetary Service. Calmly efficient executives sat at their desks, concentrating upon problems or at ease, according to the demands of the moment; agents, secretaries, and clerks, men and women, went about their wonted tasks; televisotypes and recorders flashed busily but silently﻿—each person and machine an integral part of the Service which for so many years had been carrying an ever-increasing share of the load of governing the three planets.
“Right of way, Norma?” Rodebush paused before the desk of Virgil Samms’ private secretary. She pressed a button and the door behind her swung wide.
“You two do not need to be announced,” the attractive young woman smiled. “Go right in.”
Samms met them at the door eagerly, shaking hands particularly vigorously with Cleveland.
“Congratulations on that camera, Lyman!” he exclaimed. “You did a wonderful piece of work on that. Help yourselves to smokes and sit down﻿—there are a lot of things we want to talk over. Your pictures carried most of the story, but they would have left us pretty much at sea without Costigan’s reports. But as it was, Fred here and his crew worked out most of the answers from the dope the two of you got; and what few they haven’t got yet they soon will have.”
“Nothing new on Conway?” Cleveland was almost afraid to ask the question.
“No.” A shadow came over Samms’ face. “I’m afraid﻿ ﻿… but I’m hoping it’s only that those creatures, whatever they are, have taken him so far away he can’t reach us.”
“They certainly are so far away that we can’t reach them,” Rodebush volunteered. “We can’t even get their ultra-wave interference any more.”
“Yes, that’s a hopeful sign,” Samms went on. “I hate to think of Conway Costigan checking out. There, fellows, was a real observer. He was the only man I have ever known who combined the two qualities of the perfect witness. He could actually see everything he looked at, and could report it truly, to the last, least detail.

托架电梯急坠而下，停在希尔腹地深下许多层的层面，克利夫兰与罗德布什跃出他们的座驾——舱壁犹自滚烫——穿门而入，来到一间无影日光照明的宽阔屋子：三行星服务团总长办公室。神情沉稳、干练利落的行政官各坐各的案，或凝神难题，或稍事宽弛，全看眼前需要；特工、秘书、书记员，男女皆有，各司其职；电视打字机与记录仪繁忙闪动却悄然无声——每一个人、每一台机器，都是服务团这个整体不可分的一环；多年来，治理三颗行星的重担，越挑越多的那份，正是它担着。
“可以直接进吗，诺玛？”罗德布什在弗吉尔·萨姆斯私人秘书的案前停步。她按下按钮，身后那扇门豁然大开。
“你们两位不必通传。”妩媚的年轻女子微笑道。“请进。”
萨姆斯热切地迎到门口，同克利夫兰握手握得格外用力。
“你那台摄影机，恭喜，莱曼！”他朗声道。“你干了一件了不起的活儿。烟酒自便，请坐——我们有不少事要谈。你们的影片讲出了故事的大半，可若没有康韦·科斯蒂根（Conway Costigan）的报告，我们仍不免如坠云雾。即便如此，弗雷德和他那班人已凭着你们两位搞来的材料，解出了大半答案；剩下没解出的几处，也快了。”
“康韦那边还没有新消息？”克利夫兰几乎不敢启口。
“没有。”一片阴翳掠过萨姆斯的脸。“我怕……不过我盼着，不过是那些生物——不管它们是什么——把他带得太远，远得他够不着我们。”
“他们肯定离得远，我们够不着他们。”罗德布什主动说。“我们连他们的超波干扰都收不到了。”
“是，这是吉兆。”萨姆斯接着说。“我一想到康韦·科斯蒂根就此销账就难受。那才是真正的观察员，诸位。他是我平生所知的唯一一人，兼备完美证人的两样品质。他看着什么，就真能看见什么；而且能如实上报，直到最末、最微的细节。

===Original===

Take all this stuff, for instance; especially their ability to transform iron into a fluid allotrope, and in that form to use its atomic﻿—nuclear?﻿—energy as power. Something brand new, and yet he described their converters and projectors so minutely that Fred was able to work out the underlying theory in three days, and to tie it in with our own super-ship. My first thought was that we’d have to rebuild it iron-free, but Fred showed me my error﻿—you found it first yourself, of course.”
“It wouldn’t do any good to make the ship non-ferrous unless you could so change our blood chemistry that we could get along without hemoglobin, and that would be quite a feat,” Cleveland agreed. “Then, too, our most vital electrical machinery is built around iron cores. We’ll also have to develop a screen for those forces﻿—screens, rather, so powerful that they can’t drive anything through them.”
“We’ve been working along those lines ever since you reported,” Rodebush said, “and we’re beginning to see light. And in that same connection it’s no wonder that we couldn’t handle our super-ship. We had some good ideas, but they were wrongly applied. However, things look quite promising now. We have the transformation of iron all worked out in theory, and as soon as we get a generator going we can straighten out everything else in short order. And think what that unlimited power means! All the power we want﻿—power enough even to try out such hitherto purely theoretical possibilities as the neutralization of the inertia of matter!”
“Hold on!” protested Samms. “You certainly can’t do *that*! Inertia is﻿—*must* be﻿—a basic attribute of matter, and surely cannot be done away with without destroying the matter itself. Don’t start anything like that, Fred﻿—I don’t want to lose you and Lyman, too.”
“Don’t worry about us, Chief,” Rodebush replied with a smile. “If you will tell me what matter is, fundamentally, I may agree with you.﻿ ﻿… No? Well, then, don’t be surprised at anything that happens. We are going to do a lot of things that nobody on the Three Planets ever thought of doing before.”
Thus for a long time the argument and discussion went on, to be interrupted by the voice of the secretary.
“Sorry to disturb you, Mr. Samms, but some things have come up that you will have to handle. Knobos is calling from Mars.

===Chinese===

就说这一堆东西吧；尤其是他们把铁变成流体同素异形体的本事，还有以此形态、用它的原子——核子？——能作动力的本事。全新的东西，可他把他们的换能器和投射器描述得那么细，弗雷德三天就推出了底层理论，还能同我们自己的超级飞船挂上钩。我起初想着，得把她改成全无铁质再造一遍，可弗雷德点破了我的错——这个你自然早就先看出来了。”
“把船改成无铁没什么用，除非你还能把我们的血液化学也改了，改到没有血红蛋白也过得下去，那可就是绝活了。”克利夫兰附和。“再说，我们最要紧的电机都绕着铁芯造。还有，我们得为那些力研制一种护屏——不如说一整套护屏，强到他们什么都攻不穿。”
“自你报告之后，我们就一直朝这些方向干。”罗德布什说，“而且开始看见亮了。也是在这条线上，难怪我们摆布不动我们的超级飞船。我们的点子不错，只是用错了地方。不过如今形势相当乐观。铁的转变我们已在理论上全盘吃透，等发电机一转起来，其余诸事顷刻可理。再想想那无限的动力意味着什么！我们要多少有多少——多到足以试一试那些迄今纯乎理论的可能，比如物质惯性的中和！”
“打住！”萨姆斯抗议。“你总不能干那个！惯性是——必是——物质的基本属性，若不毁掉物质本身，断无消除之理。别开这种头，弗雷德——我不想连你带莱曼一起搭进去。”
“别替我们担心，长官。”罗德布什笑着回答。“你若能告诉我，物质从根本上说是什么，我就依你。……说不出？那就不管出什么事，都别惊讶。我们要干一大堆三颗行星上谁都没想过要干的事。”
于是争论与研讨持续良久，直到秘书的声音打断。
“打搅了，萨姆斯先生，可有几件事冒了出来，非您处置不可。诺博斯从火星呼您。

===Original===

He has caught the *Endymion*, and has killed about half her crew doing it. Milton has finally reported from Venus, after being out of touch for five days. He trailed the Wintons into Thalleron swamp. They crashed him there, and he won out and has what he went after. And just now I got a flash from Fletcher, in the asteroid belt. I think that he has finally traced that dope line. But Knobos is on now﻿—what do you want him to do about the *Endymion*?”
“Tell him to﻿—no, put him on here, I’d better tell him myself,” Samms directed, and his face hardened in ruthless decision as the horny, misshapen face of the Martian lieutenant appeared upon the screen. “What do you think, Knobos? Shall they come to trial or not?”
“Not.”
“I don’t think so, either. It is better that a few gangsters should disappear in space than that the Patrol should have to put down another uprising. See to it.”
“Right.” The screen darkened and Samms spoke to his secretary. “Put Milton and Fletcher on whenever they come in.” He turned to his guests. “We’ve covered the ground quite thoroughly. Goodbye﻿—I wish I could go with you, but I’ll be pretty well tied up for the next week or two.”
“ ‘Tied up’ doesn’t half express it,” Rodebush remarked as the two scientists walked along a corridor toward an elevator. “He probably is the busiest man on three planets.”
“As well as the most powerful,” Cleveland supplemented. “And very few men could use his power as fairly﻿—but he’s welcome to it, as far as I’m concerned. I’d have the pink fantods for a month if I had to do only once what he’s just done﻿—and to him it’s just part of a day’s work.”
“You mean the *Endymion*? What else could he do?”
“Nothing﻿—that’s the hell of it. It had to be done, since bringing them to trial would mean killing half the people of Morseca; but at the same time it’s a ghastly thing to order a job of deliberate, cold-blooded, and illegal murder.”
“You’re right, of course, but you would﻿ ﻿…” he broke off, unable to put his thoughts into words. For while inarticulate, manlike, concerning their deepest emotions, in both men was ingrained the code of the organization; both knew that to every man chosen for it **the Service** was everything, himself nothing.
“But enough of that, we’ll have plenty of grief of our own right here.”

他把恩底弥翁号逮着了，逮的过程中击毙了约半数船员。弥尔顿失联五天后，总算从金星报了信。他一路跟踪温顿家进了塔勒隆沼泽。他们在那儿把他摔了下来，可他赢了，要去的东西也到手了。方才我又收到小行星带里弗莱彻的急讯。我想他总算摸到那条毒线了。不过诺博斯在线上——恩底弥翁号的事，您要他怎么办？”
“告诉他——不，把线转过来，我最好亲自对他说。”萨姆斯吩咐。火星人中尉那张粗糙多疣的怪脸一上屏，他的脸便在无情的决断中冷硬下来。“你怎么看，诺博斯？送他们上审判，还是不送？”
“不送。”
“我也不想送。让几个匪徒消失在太空里，总好过巡逻队再去平一场暴动。照此办理。”
“是。”屏幕暗下，萨姆斯吩咐秘书：“弥尔顿和弗莱彻一上线就接给我。”他转向客人。“要谈的都谈透了。再见——我真想同去，可这一两个星期我分身乏术。”
“‘分身乏术’还说轻了。”两位科学家沿走廊走向电梯时罗德布什说。“他多半是三颗行星上最忙的人。”
“也是最有力量的。”克利夫兰补上一句。“而他那份力量，用得比他公道的没几人——不过让我说，这份力量他尽管留着。他方才干的那号事，哪怕只叫我干上一回，我也得心惊肉跳一个月——在他，却不过是份内一日。”
“你是说恩底弥翁号？他还能怎么办？”
“没法办——要命就在这儿。这事非办不可，因为押他们受审，就等于杀死摩尔塞卡一半的人；可与此同时，下令一桩蓄意、冷血、又非法的谋杀，又是何等阴森的一件事。”
“你说得当然对，可你会……”他顿住了，想不出话来。两人虽同一切凡人一样，最深的情感难以出口，组织的那条信条却早已刻进两人骨血；两人都知道，凡入选之人，服务团便是一切，自身则什么也不是。

“不说这个了，咱们自己这份苦头也够受的，就在这儿。”跨进一间巨大的舱室时，罗德布什陡然转了话头。那舱室几乎被博伊西号庞大的躯体塞满——那艘凶名昭著的太空船，虽从未升空，却早已在三行星的名册上抹黑了不知多少页。不过此刻，它是狂热忙碌的中心。人们爬满她的全身、穿行她的内外，在那份争分夺秒又环环严扣的重建纲领中，秩序井然地一片忙乱。
